| Comoros | A [national interoperable payment switch went live on 23 August 2025](https://pay-logic.com/paylogic-announces-the-go-live-of-the-comoross-national-interoperable-payment-switch), and a [second switch launched in May 2026](https://banque-comores.km/article/show/ceremonie-de-lancement-des-nouveaux-moyens-de-paiement). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The switch was [awarded to a supplier on 16 February 2025](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/comoros-deploys-digital-payment-switch) and [went live on 23 August 2025 connecting commercial banks, microfinance institutions and mobile-money operators](https://pay-logic.com/paylogic-announces-the-go-live-of-the-comoross-national-interoperable-payment-switch), [reported by two outlets](https://techafricanews.com/2025/08/27/paylogic-launches-national-payment-switch-in-comoros-to-boost-digital-financial-inclusion/).<br><br>2026-05-04 - the central bank launched a switch on a [three-phase plan - cross-bank cash withdrawal, then instant bank to mobile-money transfer, then merchant acceptance - with the first phase from July 2026 and online purchases explicitly not covered](https://banque-comores.km/uploads/BCC-livret%283%29.pdf).<br><br>Whether the two are the same object is unsettled: one account tags the same supplier and nothing on file names a vendor for the later system.</details> | Advanced |
| Congo | A [payment aggregator was licensed for a unified interface across mobile operators, banks and microfinance institutions](https://techafricanews.com/2026/06/16/pawapay-subsidiary-kerry-payments-secures-vas-payment-aggregator-licence-in-congo/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-06 - a regulator licence was [secured for a unified interface across mobile operators, banks and microfinance institutions, the first concrete test of the mobile-money co-regulation question the industry has flagged](https://techafricanews.com/2026/06/16/pawapay-subsidiary-kerry-payments-secures-vas-payment-aggregator-licence-in-congo/).<br><br>No transaction volume, pricing or count of connected institutions is published. The co-regulation question it tests - which regulator supervises a service that is at once telecommunications and payment - has no answer on this ledger, and no memorandum between the telecommunications and financial regulators appears anywhere.</details> | Advanced |
| Cote d'Ivoire | A [cross-network transfer application went live in May 2026](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/tech-stars/avec-simocash-l-ivoirien-wilfried-kouame-interconnecte-les-reseaux-d-argent-mobile), and [school fees enter a mobile-money pilot](https://www.linfodrome.com/societe/124130-frais-de-scolarite-par-mobile-money-la-feneplaci-passe-a-la-phase-test-dans-200-etablissements). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-05 - a [privately built application moving funds directly between mobile-money networks went live in six West African countries with instant cross-border transfers](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/tech-stars/avec-simocash-l-ivoirien-wilfried-kouame-interconnecte-les-reseaux-d-argent-mobile). Its volumes, licensing and regulatory status are not stated.<br><br>2026-07-21 - a convention opened a [pilot in 200 private secular schools from August 2026: fee collection through a mobile wallet, credit lines to schools and fee pre-financing for parents up to FCFA 1 million](https://www.linfodrome.com/societe/124130-frais-de-scolarite-par-mobile-money-la-feneplaci-passe-a-la-phase-test-dans-200-etablissements). No fee volume, pilot duration, pricing or data-handling arrangement is disclosed, and a pre-financing facility attached to school enrolment has no consumer-credit safeguard on file.</details> | Advanced |
| Djibouti | The [mobile wallet has been in service since June 2020](https://www.lanation.dj/lancement-de-la-premiere-plateforme-en-ligne-a-djibouti-developpe-par-djibouti-telecom-a-travers-d-money/) and its online payment gateway since December 2023. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The wallet has been [in service since June 2020, with an online payment gateway launched under the Prime Minister's patronage on 10 December 2023](https://www.lanation.dj/lancement-de-la-premiere-plateforme-en-ligne-a-djibouti-developpe-par-djibouti-telecom-a-travers-d-money/) and [extended to the ports](https://developingtelecoms.com/telecom-technology/financial-services/15938-digital-money-transfer-system-comes-to-djibouti-s-ports.html).<br><br>Neither moved in the window and no subscriber, wallet or transaction figure has ever been published for either. The conventions signed on the gateway's launch day are named by category only and are not held. Both are the incumbent's, in a market with no competing licensed operator.</details> | No change |
| DR Congo | The state bus operator opened a QR-code ticketing trial in Kinshasa ([trial](https://acp.cd/business/kinshasa-la-vente-des-produits-maraichers-presentee-comme-un-levier-de-renforcement-financier/)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The state bus operator opened a trial of QR-code electronic ticketing in Kinshasa on 31 August 2026 ([trial](https://acp.cd/business/kinshasa-la-vente-des-produits-maraichers-presentee-comme-un-levier-de-renforcement-financier/)). No fare volume, route coverage or payment provider is named.<br><br>The interoperability service has been [live since the fourth quarter of 2025 and is now the rail on which a stablecoin settlement use case runs](https://visa-inc.africa-newsroom.com/press/visa-pay-launches-to-expand-access-to-digital-payments-across-the-democratic-republic-of-the-congo-drc); no transaction volume, merchant count or user figure is held.<br><br>2026-04-30 - a [palm-vein payment technology launched in Kinshasa, stated as integrated into national payments infrastructure and named against the 2023-2028 financial-inclusion strategy](https://actualite.cd/2026/04/30/rdc-lancement-de-mainmoney-une-technologie-monetaire-pour-eviter-la-population-du-poids), with [no deployment count or transaction figure](https://www.biometricupdate.com/202605/mainmoney-palm-biometrics-platform-to-support-drcs-financial-inclusion-drive).<br><br>2026-07-14 - a card network and a domestic payments company [announced physical and virtual prepaid card issuance, the domestic partner serving more than three million customers through 4,000 agents and 160 agencies](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/14/mastercard-and-flash-partner-to-expand-digital-financial-inclusion-in-drc/); no issuance date or card volume is stated.</details> | Advanced |
| Egypt | The central bank [launched acceptance on merchants' own phones](https://www.dailynewsegypt.com/2026/02/01/cbe-launches-soft-pos-service-for-contactless-mobile-payments-to-cut-merchant-costs), and a [first carrier-billing licence covers government payments](https://tpaymobile.com/tpay-secures-license-to-offer-direct-mobile-payments-for-government-services-in-egypt). <details><summary>Full record</summary>Phone-based acceptance was [a pilot capped at EGP 600 per transaction](https://www.dailynewsegypt.com/2026/02/01/cbe-launches-soft-pos-service-for-contactless-mobile-payments-to-cut-merchant-costs) and is now live: merchants may use a smartphone or tablet as an electronic point of sale, accepting contactless card payments with the customer entering a PIN on the device. No merchant count, transaction volume or cap for the live service is held.<br><br>2025-12-11 - a [first regulator licence for direct carrier billing was signed, letting citizens pay utility bills, traffic fines and civil-registry charges from mobile balance or the monthly bill](https://tpaymobile.com/tpay-secures-license-to-offer-direct-mobile-payments-for-government-services-in-egypt). No transaction volume, participating operator list or live-service date is held after signature.</details> | Advanced |
| Equatorial Guinea | Five commercial banks and the regional central bank were [pressed to expand cash machines, terminals and card issuance](https://realequatorialguinea.com/destacado/politica/nguema-obiang-mangue-exige-a-la-banca-ampliar-cajeros-y-tpv-para-reducir-el-uso-de-efectivo/) in February 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The expansion is sought for value-added tax control and tourism, and no deployment count has been published ([pressure](https://realequatorialguinea.com/destacado/politica/nguema-obiang-mangue-exige-a-la-banca-ampliar-cajeros-y-tpv-para-reducir-el-uso-de-efectivo/)).<br><br>A national plan for cash machines and terminals was launched in May 2025, with connectivity named by the banks and the state infrastructure operator as the limiting factor ([plan](https://news.identicge.org/guinea-ecuatorial-apuesta-por-la-transformacion-digital-cajeros-automaticos-y-tpvs/)).<br><br>So the country has a plan, a ministerial push and no count of machines or terminals at either date.</details> | Advanced, with no delivery figure |
| Eritrea | There is no digital payment rail of any kind; a modernisation project remains prospective ([mission](https://www.afdb.org/en/news-and-events/press-releases/african-development-bank-and-eritrea-deepen-partnership-rooted-trust-and-pragmatism-93081)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>A development bank delegation visited in April 2026 and reviewed continued dialogue on a prospective financial sector modernisation project, with discussions covering financial-sector modernisation and data systems ([mission](https://www.afdb.org/en/news-and-events/press-releases/african-development-bank-and-eritrea-deepen-partnership-rooted-trust-and-pragmatism-93081)). The project is prospective, not approved.<br><br>The system it would modernise has no cash machines, debit or credit cards, or internet banking ([evaluation](https://www.esaamlg.org/reports/MER%20of%20Eritrea-July%202025.pdf)), and Eritrea does not appear in the continental instant-payments inventory ([inventory](https://www.africanenda.org/uploads/files/siips_2024_mainreport_EN.1.pdf)).<br><br>Person-to-person, person-to-government and person-to-business payment are all cash transactions here, and the base records no exception.</details> | No change |
| Eswatini | The [payment switch launched fast payments](https://www.bis.org/review/r250114f.htm) as phase one of three, and [35 post offices opened for government-service payments](https://techafricanews.com/2025/09/26/eswatini-ministry-of-ict-unveils-post-office-based-government-service-payment-system/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>Fast payments launched as phase one of three; no source held confirms whether the remaining five participants went live as expected by end-January 2025 ([launch](https://www.bis.org/review/r250114f.htm), [explainer](https://www.times.co.sz/personal-finance/readmore.php?bhsadjgfoh=Eswatini+Payment+Switch+making+payments+faster%2C+easier&bvhdgsj=Personal+Finance&yiphi=408)).<br><br>Thirty-five post offices were opened for government-service payments in September 2025 ([post offices](https://techafricanews.com/2025/09/26/eswatini-ministry-of-ict-unveils-post-office-based-government-service-payment-system/)).<br><br>A rail whose participant list cannot be confirmed eighteen months after phase one, alongside a physical over-the-counter channel, is where person-to-government payment actually stands here.</details> | Advanced |
| Ethiopia | An [instant payment system](https://cioafrica.co/ethiopia-launches-national-instant-payments/) launched in February 2026 across 32 banks and 12 microfinance institutions, with merchant and cash-out services added since. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The national instant payment system carries account-to-account and wallet-to-wallet transfers with a national quick-response standard and aliases ([instant payments](https://cioafrica.co/ethiopia-launches-national-instant-payments/)).<br><br>The switch behind it links the challenger wallet to more than 30 banks and wallets in real time, with merchant payments at more than 50,000 merchants ([switch integration](https://itweb.africa/article/m-pesa-ethiopia-surges-258-as-ethswitch-fuels-growth/)).<br><br>Cardless cash withdrawals with one bank went live in August 2026, authorised in the app before cash is dispensed ([cardless withdrawals](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/11/m-pesa-ethiopia-launches-cardless-atm-withdrawals-with-bank-of-abyssinia/)), and prepaid electricity became purchasable in the wallet in July ([electricity payments](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/29/m-pesa-ethiopia-launches-prepaid-electricity-payments-through-super-app/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Gabon | A [national QR payment scheme](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1112-51310-gabon-speeds-up-digital-payments-rollout-with-madigipaie-program) launched in December 2025 with more than 1,000 codes, and the regional switch moved 20.3m transactions in 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The scheme was launched at Libreville by the regional switch operator, presided over by the central bank governor, with more than 1,000 codes at launch ([launch](https://gimac-afr.com/2025/12/09/cemac-madigipaie-revolutionne-leconomie-numerique/), [scheme](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1112-51310-gabon-speeds-up-digital-payments-rollout-with-madigipaie-program)).<br><br>It extended to the taxi network on 28 February 2026 ([taxi extension](https://www.gabonreview.com/gabon-madigipaie-fait-de-taxigab-le-fer-de-lance-du-paiement-numerique/)) and municipal facilitation was agreed on 3 July 2026 ([municipal agreement](https://union.sonapresse.com/fr/paiement-numerique-la-mairie-de-libreville-accompagne-le-deploiement-de-madigipaie)); no transaction volume, value or merchant count above the launch codes has been published since.<br><br>The regional switch itself carried 20.3m transactions worth FCFA 863bn across 153 participants in 2025, against a reconstructed 14.5m worth about FCFA 710bn in 2024 ([switch figures](https://leconomie.info/article/paiements-numeriques-gimacpay-enregistre-203-millions-de-transactions-pour-863-milliards-de-fcfa-en-2025)).</details> | Advanced |
| Gambia | An [instant payment system](https://thepoint.gm/africa/gambia/headlines/gambia-launches-bantaba-2-0-first-real-time-interoperable-payment-system) launched in December 2025 across banks, mobile money, microfinance and fintechs. <details><summary>Full record</summary>It is built on an open-source core and operated by the national switch; no transaction volume has been published ([launch](https://thepoint.gm/africa/gambia/headlines/gambia-launches-bantaba-2-0-first-real-time-interoperable-payment-system)).<br><br>A partnership signed in June 2025 to take public transport fares by mobile money is unchanged, with no deployment, route or volume figure published in the fourteen months since ([transport fares](https://thepoint.gm/africa/gambia/national-news/wave-gambia-gtsc-launch-digital-payment-partnership)).</details> | Advanced |
| Ghana | [GH¢492.9bn moved on 954m transactions in June 2026 alone](https://www.myjoyonline.com/mobile-money-transactions-hit-gh%c2%a2492-9bn-in-june/), up about 52 per cent on June 2025, with interoperable traffic at about 1.3 per cent of value. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The full year 2023 carried 6.81bn transactions worth GH¢1.92tn, so a single month of 2026 is a quarter of that year's value ([June data](https://www.myjoyonline.com/mobile-money-transactions-hit-gh%c2%a2492-9bn-in-june/), [oversight report](https://www.bog.gov.gh/news/payment-systems-oversight-annual-report-2023/)).<br><br>Interoperability has been live since 2018 and carried 33.5m transactions worth GH¢6.2bn in June 2026, about 1.3 per cent of total mobile-money value ([interoperability](https://www.newsghana.com.gh/ghana-mobile-money-interoperability-nearly-doubles-in-2025/)); the share, not the volume, is the measure of it ([interoperability](https://www.newsghana.com.gh/ghana-mobile-money-interoperability-nearly-doubles-in-2025/)).<br><br>The dominant operator's platform moved off a legacy virtualised environment onto a cloud-native vendor platform, the vendor reporting cuts of up to 86 per cent in processing and database load and up to 80 per cent in response times, its own figures with no downtime, cost or subscriber-impact figure stated ([migration](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/21/ericsson-mtn-complete-momo-cloud-migration-across-four-african-markets/)).<br><br>A rival launched an agent commission structure paying more for higher transacted value before more than 400 agents and retailers, the bands unpublished ([agent scheme](https://www.citinewsroom.com/2026/08/telecel-rolls-out-agent-loyalty-scheme-to-strengthen-mobile-money-ecosystem/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Guinea | A [national instant payment system](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/2807-57779-guinea-launches-nimbapay-its-national-instant-payment-system) opened in July 2026, interoperable across banks, electronic-money institutions and microfinance. <details><summary>Full record</summary>It was built in 127 days on an open-source core by the national card company with a continental payments foundation and a technical partner, and went live nationwide on 22 July 2026 ([launch](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/2807-57779-guinea-launches-nimbapay-its-national-instant-payment-system), [opening](https://mediaguinee.com/2026/07/le-pm-amadou-oury-bah-lance-les-operations-de-la-plateforme-nimbapay-initiee-par-la-bcrg-nous-devons-rattraper-notre-retard/)).<br><br>The separate national switch project, memorandum signed in December 2024, is not operational as a switch in its own right ([project register](https://www.bcrg.gn/projets-de-reformes-en-cours-a-travers-la-dsmp/)).<br><br>No transaction volume, value or participant count has been published in the month since the system opened.</details> | Advanced |
| Guinea-Bissau | The country gained its [first licensed payment institution](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2025-09/LISTE_DES_ETABLISSEMENTS_DE_PAIEMENT_AGREES_15_septembre_2025.pdf) in September 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Retail electronic payment capacity is new and small: the [first payment institution licensed under the monetary union appeared on the register in September 2025](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2025-09/LISTE_DES_ETABLISSEMENTS_DE_PAIEMENT_AGREES_15_septembre_2025.pdf), and by [June 2026 the country had one payment institution and one electronic money issuer](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/inline-files/4%20-%20Liste%20Guin%C3%A9e-Bissau_2026.pdf).<br><br>Person-to-government payment is the mature side, through the [simplified tax and contribution payment platform](https://kontaktu.mef.gw/instructions) the revenue directorate has made compulsory.</details> | Advanced |
| Kenya | The [dominant wallet's business tariffs were cut](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/04/safaricom-lowers-m-pesa-fees-expands-data-and-fibre-benefits-under-pata-more/) from 7 August 2026, with merchant payments fee-free to KES 500 and transfer bands down by up to 49.5 per cent. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Merchant receipts are fee-free to KES 500 from KES 200, and the small-trader product to KES 200 from KES 100 with fees capped for 90 days; it was delivered as voluntary alignment with central bank pricing principles, with no legal notice or gazetted schedule to cite ([tariffs](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/04/safaricom-lowers-m-pesa-fees-expands-data-and-fibre-benefits-under-pata-more/)).<br><br>A single application replaced the separate wallet and account apps in April 2026, with the standalone apps to be decommissioned six months after launch ([single app](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/02/safaricom-introduces-my-oneapp/)).<br><br>The second operator launched a dedicated business wallet, free to fund and returning half of transaction fees as in-wallet cashback, on a 10.9 per cent mobile-money share ([business wallet](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/03/airtel-targets-kenyas-small-businesses-as-mobile-money-war-shifts-to-merchants/)).<br><br>Alongside the mobile-money rails, a bank-owned scheme runs account-to-account instant transfers around the clock [across more than 80 financial institutions](https://pesalink.co.ke/), reached through internet banking, bank applications and short codes, with a ceiling of KSh999,999 a transfer. The interoperability claim is the operator's own and no transaction volume, value or pricing schedule is published on the page held.</details> | Advanced |
| Lesotho | A [wallet investment service](https://www.connectingafrica.com/fintech/vodacom-lesotho-launches-m-pesa-investment-solution) launched in July 2026 over USSD, from M50 for individual customers and M300 for group-savings customers. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Tsetela launched in Maseru on 22 July 2026 over a USSD short code, from M50 for individual mobile-money customers and M300 for group-savings customers, with the asset manager licensed by the Central Bank of Lesotho ([launch](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/vodacomlesotho_mpesa-tsetela-financialinclusion-activity-7485718047811489792-WV1A), [account](https://lestimes.com/?p=91054)).<br><br>It is the first investment product reachable from a wallet on record ([report](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/24/vodacom-lesotho-launches-tsetela-investment-service-on-m-pesa/)), and no subscriber or value figure has been published since ([coverage](https://www.connectingafrica.com/fintech/vodacom-lesotho-launches-m-pesa-investment-solution)).</details> | Advanced |
| Liberia | An [inclusive instant payment system](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/africanenda_bringing-every-stakeholder-to-the-table-isnt-activity-7494679350248820736--7a1) launched in December 2025 in 73 business days, interoperable between the two mobile-money networks. <details><summary>Full record</summary>It covers person-to-person and government-to-person payments, where mobile money had been split between two closed networks ([launch](https://frontpageafricaonline.com/business/economy/liberia-cbl-launches-inclusive-instant-payment-system-ends-era-of-mobile-money-silos/), [system](https://techafricanews.com/2025/12/16/liberia-launches-inclusive-instant-payment-system-to-enable-real-time-transfers/)).<br><br>The central bank's own account is that it was built to a business case rather than to a regulation and sequenced government payments first, moving civil-servant salaries off cheques into wallets and so giving the integrated operators access to float, against a national target of 50 per cent of adults holding an account ([central bank account](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/africanenda_bringing-every-stakeholder-to-the-table-isnt-activity-7494679350248820736--7a1)).<br><br>A national electronic payment switch is named as the follow-on, alongside person-to-government payments, with no go-live date or budget line published ([switch](https://www.liberianobserver.com/business/gov-t-targets-24-hour-digital-economy-to-boost-youth-creativity/article_29307cdc-ea0a-44aa-962a-2c66f40209ec.html), [expansion](https://www.thenewdawnliberia.com/liberia-expands-digital-finance/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Libya | [Instant transfers](https://alwasat.ly/news/libya/509053) ran 18.3m times in 2025, and acceptance widened to [petrol stations](https://libyaherald.com/2026/01/jumhuria-bank-launches-libyas-first-e-payment-service-for-petrol-stations/) and to [phones used as terminals](https://libyaherald.com/2026/06/central-bank-of-libya-governor-inaugurates-2nd-libya-international-electronic-payment-exhibition-epaix-2026). <details><summary>Full record</summary>LYPAY ONEPAY carried 18.3m transfers worth LD 108.7bn in 2025, the first published volumes for the service ([volumes](https://alwasat.ly/news/libya/509053)).<br><br>Jumhouria Bank brought card acceptance to petrol stations on 15 January 2026, the first such service ([petrol stations](https://libyaherald.com/2026/01/jumhuria-bank-launches-libyas-first-e-payment-service-for-petrol-stations/)).<br><br>SoftPOS was launched at EPAIX on 17 June 2026, letting NFC phones act as card terminals ([launch](https://libyaherald.com/2026/06/central-bank-of-libya-governor-inaugurates-2nd-libya-international-electronic-payment-exhibition-epaix-2026), [conference](https://alwasat.ly/news/libya/521750)). No count of active wallets, or of merchants accepting by either route, is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Madagascar | A [bank was integrated into a mobile money service](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/17/airtel-money-madagascar-and-baobab-bank-partner-to-expand-mobile-banking-services/) in July 2026, giving bidirectional wallet-to-account transfers over short code and application. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The integration gives bidirectional transfers between wallet and bank account over unstructured supplementary service data and the bank's own application ([integration](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/17/airtel-money-madagascar-and-baobab-bank-partner-to-expand-mobile-banking-services/)).<br><br>It is the base's only record of a wallet and a bank interoperating in the country. No transfer volume, fee or count of participating banks is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Malawi | Mobile money was [integrated into the agricultural marketing corporation](https://itweb.africa/article/airtel-digitises-malawian-farmer-payments/JN1gPvOAE4DMjL6m) so farmers are paid into wallets, and [card e-commerce acceptance](https://itweb.africa/article/standard-bank-expands-unionpay-acceptance/KBpdgvpmBA47LEew) opened to merchants. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The integration lets farmers be paid directly into mobile wallets and customers pay electronically for commodities across the corporation's national market and depot network; no volume, value or farmer count is held ([integration](https://itweb.africa/article/airtel-digitises-malawian-farmer-payments/JN1gPvOAE4DMjL6m)).<br><br>Online acceptance of an international card scheme opened to participating merchants in August 2026, settling in local currency, dollars or both, as part of an extension to nine African markets; no Malawi-specific merchant count is given ([acceptance](https://itweb.africa/article/standard-bank-expands-unionpay-acceptance/KBpdgvpmBA47LEew), [extension](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/19/unionpay-and-standard-bank-expand-e-commerce-acceptance-across-nine-african-markets/)).<br><br>The incumbent operator's mobile-money platform reported MK51.93bn of revenue in 2025, up 77 per cent on the year, on a stated base of more than 140,000 active agents ([annual report](https://malawi24.com/2026/08/24/can-tnms-mk30-87bn-investment-meet-demand/)) - its first published revenue line, with no account, transaction or value figure alongside it.</details> | Advanced |
| Mali | Both mobile money operators now issue prepaid cards: [one launched in June 2025](https://www.maliweb.net/economie/cooperation/monnaie-numerique-moderniser-les-paiements-3105976.html) with transaction caps, the [second in April 2026](https://afrique.le360.ma/economie/gitex-africa-2026-a-marrakech-moov-money-mali-lance-sa-carte-prepayee-co-brandee-en-partenariat-avec_BSK7BUJUVFG2PCVQA4FSPVIXRA/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The first telco-led prepaid card launched with a bank partner in June 2025, capped at two million francs a transaction and ten million a month ([first card](https://www.maliweb.net/economie/cooperation/monnaie-numerique-moderniser-les-paiements-3105976.html)).<br><br>The second launched at a regional technology exhibition in April 2026 ([second card](https://afrique.le360.ma/economie/gitex-africa-2026-a-marrakech-moov-money-mali-lance-sa-carte-prepayee-co-brandee-en-partenariat-avec_BSK7BUJUVFG2PCVQA4FSPVIXRA/)). No issuance, activation or spend figure is published for either, so what the base records is that the wallet has acquired a card rail and not how much travels on it.</details> | Advanced |
| Mauritius | The [instant payment switch](https://www.africanenda.org/uploads/files/siips_2024_MauCas_CaseStudy_en.pdf) is a mature interoperable rail with a code standard, and the largest bank reports [code payments up 67 per cent](https://lexpress.mu/node/553532) with cash down to 27 per cent of consumer spend. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The switch was profiled as a mature interoperable national rail with a code standard in a 2024 instant-payments review, and no national transaction volume has been published since ([switch](https://www.africanenda.org/uploads/files/siips_2024_MauCas_CaseStudy_en.pdf)).<br><br>The largest bank's 2025 figures put cash at 27 per cent of consumer spend against 30 per cent a year earlier, code payments up 67 per cent year on year, contactless above six in ten card transactions and about 18,000 merchants accepting ([adoption](https://lexpress.mu/node/553532)); they are one bank's figures rather than a national series ([adoption](https://lexpress.mu/node/553532)).</details> | Advanced |
| Morocco | A [merchant acquisition fund grant](https://d-portal.iatistandard.org/ctrack.html#view=act&aid=46002-P-MA-H00-032) of about US$505,704 was committed for 2026 to 2028 through a continental digital financial inclusion facility. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The activity was reported at pipeline stage in February 2026, committing XDR 370,023.73, about US$505,704 at the provisional annual average rate, over 2026 to 2028 ([activity](https://d-portal.iatistandard.org/ctrack.html#view=act&aid=46002-P-MA-H00-032)).<br><br>It responds to challenges the activity attributes to the central bank, and no disbursement transaction is reported. No merchant acceptance count or point-of-sale estate figure is held for the country.</details> | Advanced |
| Mozambique | An [instant payment system](https://www.bancomoc.mz/media/1bwihts1/aviso-1-gbm-2026.pdf) entered production in March 2026 with free interbank person-to-person transfers, and [daily transfers tripled](https://360mozambique.com/economy/banking/metix-instant-payment-system-surpasses-11000-daily-transfers/) to more than 11,000. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The system runs around the clock over bank applications, websites and short codes, operated by the interbank company with wallet-operator participation mandatory and free interbank person-to-person transfers for individuals; the governing notice sets daily limits of 200,000 metical for individuals and 500,000 for legal persons and a twenty-five second fund-availability ceiling ([notice](https://www.bancomoc.mz/media/1bwihts1/aviso-1-gbm-2026.pdf), [launch](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/central-bank-launches-mozambique-instant-payment-system-metix-watch/)).<br><br>Daily transfers passed 11,000 by July 2026, a tripling since production entry in March ([volumes](https://360mozambique.com/economy/banking/metix-instant-payment-system-surpasses-11000-daily-transfers/)). Against a base of more than twenty-five million wallet accounts, that is a system working and barely used.<br><br>The switch behind it is not new: the interbank company was established in 2011 to give all payment service providers a single clearance platform, launched a bank-to-bank instant payment system in 2012 and a cross-domain system in 2022, before which processors and switches were not interconnected, pricing was opaque and interoperability between bank accounts and mobile money was limited ([case study](https://www.africanenda.org/uploads/files/siips2025/siips_2025_SIMO-Mozambique_CaseStudy_en.pdf)).</details> | Advanced |
| Namibia | An [Instant Payment Solution](https://thebrief.com.na/2026/07/namibia-becomes-fourth-african-country-to-launch-instant-payments-system/) went live in June 2026 with three participating institutions, a [declaration of intent](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/02/bank-of-namibia-ipn-and-namclear-sign-pact-to-strengthen-instant-payments/) was signed on clearing integration, and a [quick-response standard](https://www.namibiansun.com/mw-main/namibia-moves-closer-to-instant-payments-NMH014446-3015-17893) is finalised. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The solution became operational in June 2026 after a live pilot, with a phased national rollout; the go-live month is stated by the central bank and no day is given ([launch](https://thebrief.com.na/2026/07/namibia-becomes-fourth-african-country-to-launch-instant-payments-system/), [build](https://neweralive.na/namibia-draws-closer-to-instant-government-payments)). It is carried publicly as WayaMe, operated by Instant Payments Namibia under the central bank, customers transacting through their existing bank applications, wallets or USSD while the payment is routed through the national infrastructure, and it is designed to carry bank-to-bank, bank-to-wallet, wallet-to-bank and wallet-to-wallet payments ([name](https://www.namibian.com.na/explainer-what-is-wayame/)). No participant list, volume, value or fee schedule is published.<br><br>A declaration of intent was signed by the central bank, the instant payments company and the clearing house to integrate instant payments with the national clearing infrastructure through the interbank settlement system, with no deliverables, funding or timetable published ([declaration](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/02/bank-of-namibia-ipn-and-namclear-sign-pact-to-strengthen-instant-payments/)).<br><br>The national quick-response payment standard is finalised with operationalisation named a priority and no live deployment reported ([standard](https://thebrief.com.na/2026/07/namibia-becomes-fourth-african-country-to-launch-instant-payments-system/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Niger | [Instant bank-to-wallet transfers](https://www.lesahel.org/communique-de-presse-niger-banque-atlantique-et-nita-renforcent-linteroperabilite-entre-services-bancaires-et-paiement-mobile/) went live in June 2026 between one bank's accounts and mobile wallets. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The service launched on 26 June 2026 ([transfers](https://www.lesahel.org/communique-de-presse-niger-banque-atlantique-et-nita-renforcent-linteroperabilite-entre-services-bancaires-et-paiement-mobile/)).<br><br>No volume, value or count of participating institutions is published, and one bank connecting to the wallet rails is the whole of the interoperability evidence for the country.</details> | Advanced |
| Nigeria | [Instant payments reached US$256bn](https://itweb.africa/article/nigerian-e-payments-hit-256bn/WnxpE74YLgmMV8XL) across 4.12bn transactions, a [National Payment Stack](https://theeagleonline.com.ng/nibss-rolls-out-nps-as-nip-successor/) carries 26.55m transactions on the new standard, and [short-code billing](https://techcabal.com/2025/06/11/new-ussd-billing-model-charges/) settled a multi-year dispute. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Electronic payments reached US$256bn by July 2025 from US$187bn a year earlier across 4.12bn transactions, and the instant system was rated the first African instant system to achieve mature inclusivity ([payments](https://itweb.africa/article/nigerian-e-payments-hit-256bn/WnxpE74YLgmMV8XL), [rating](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2025/12/nigeria-leads-as-africas-instant-payment-transactions-hit-2tn-siips/)).<br><br>The successor stack reports 26.55 million transactions worth ₦1.4 trillion across 48 institutions in its early phase, on a multi-currency platform carrying payments, identity and data together, with its first live transaction in November 2025 ([stack](https://theeagleonline.com.ng/nibss-rolls-out-nps-as-nip-successor/), [first transaction](https://www.thisdaylive.com/2025/11/10/nibss-executes-first-transaction-on-national-payment-stack/)).<br><br>Short-code session costs are billed to airtime at ₦6.98 per 120 seconds with real-time confirmation and opt-in, ending the multi-year operator and bank debt dispute ([billing](https://techcabal.com/2025/06/11/new-ussd-billing-model-charges/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Rwanda | The instant transfer service [was launched nationally in December 2025](https://www.risa.gov.rw/news-detail/rwanda-launches-ekash-advancing-rwandas-cashless-economy-vision) after reaching [2.1 million active users and RWF 37.7 billion processed](https://www.ktpress.rw/2025/08/bnrs-ekash-system-linking-banks-and-telecoms-sees-300-growth/) by June 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Transaction value grew 301 per cent year on year to RWF 37.7 billion by June 2025, across 20 institutions and 2.1 million active users ([report](https://www.ktpress.rw/2025/08/bnrs-ekash-system-linking-banks-and-telecoms-sees-300-growth/), [annual report](https://www.bnr.rw/documents/Annual_Report_2024_25_English_Full_Version_RwYAWN0.pdf)).<br><br>The service was launched nationally on 5 December 2025 as a digital public good connecting banks and mobile-money platforms, framed as improving government service delivery ([launch](https://www.risa.gov.rw/news-detail/rwanda-launches-ekash-advancing-rwandas-cashless-economy-vision)).<br><br>The financial inclusion roadmap treats bulk government-to-person payments, person-to-government payments and further onboarding as an active track rather than a completed one ([roadmap](https://www.bnr.rw/documents/National_Financial_Inclusion_Roadmap_2026-2030.pdf)).</details> | Advanced |
| Sierra Leone | A [national instant payment switch](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/finance/sierra-leone-launches-national-instant-payment-system) launched in February 2025 with full integration from April, and a [payments platform with a messaging chatbot and code settlement](https://owlpress-sl.com/fintech-firm-open-hub-launches-flot-platform-boost-digital-payments-sierra-leone/) launched in April 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The instant phase launched on 13 February 2025 with full integration from 1 April 2025 ([switch](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/finance/sierra-leone-launches-national-instant-payment-system)).<br><br>The payments platform launched in April 2026 with a messaging-app chatbot and code-based settlement ([platform](https://owlpress-sl.com/fintech-firm-open-hub-launches-flot-platform-boost-digital-payments-sierra-leone/)).<br><br>No transaction volume, participant count or availability figure is published for the switch at any date, so what the rail actually carries is unmeasured.</details> | Advanced |
| Somalia | The [instant payment system](https://centralbank.gov.so/nps/) added real-time person-to-person, merchant and government payments across banks, wallets and terminals in January 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The instant payment system launched in January 2025 on the 2021 clearing and settlement foundation, enabling real-time person-to-person, person-to-merchant and government-to-person transactions across banks, mobile wallets and card terminals ([description](https://centralbank.gov.so/nps/)).<br><br>A national QR standard sits beside it, and the dominant mobile-money service carries the volume the formal rails are being built to reach.<br><br>The legal framework under which all of this operates is still the 2020 mobile money regulations; the bill that would replace them passed both chambers by January 2026 and no signature is recorded ([vote](https://sonna.so/en/somalias-upper-house-passes-national-payment-system-bill-receives-briefing-on-worsening-drought/)).</details> | Advanced |
| South Africa | The instant payment rail reached [905m cumulative transactions by May 2026](https://techcentral.co.za/three-years-in-payshap-pivots-to-merchants/281687/), a [national QR code standard reached a v1.2 eight-bank cohort](https://www.bizcommunity.com/article/massive-payment-change-sarbs-qr-could-simplify-checkout-nationwide-620801a), and merger conditions [opened one bank's rail to third parties](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/compcom-approves-capitecs-walletdoc-acquisition/Olx4z7kaoeyq56km). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-05 — the instant payment rail [went from 461m cumulative transactions in March 2026 to 905m by May and pivoted toward merchants](https://techcentral.co.za/three-years-in-payshap-pivots-to-merchants/281687/), having [passed R100bn in value](https://clearingpost.com/insights/payshap-three-years-r100b-sarb-national-utility/). Payments is the fastest-moving part of this ledger by a wide margin.<br><br>2026-07 — a [national payment code standard reached version 1.2 with an eight-bank test cohort](https://www.bizcommunity.com/article/massive-payment-change-sarbs-qr-could-simplify-checkout-nationwide-620801a).<br><br>2026-08-06 — the competition authority [recommended approval of a bank's R400 million acquisition of a payment provider subject to behavioural conditions](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/compcom-approves-capitecs-walletdoc-acquisition/Olx4z7kaoeyq56km): the merged entity must make its pay service available to third-party providers technically and commercially capable of integrating with it, may not use confidential information obtained through that access to compete against them, and must treat competing rails no less favourably unless objectively justified. Access to a dominant rail is being set by merger condition rather than by rule, which is a different thing from an access regime and binds only one party.<br><br>2026-08-31 — a proprietary QR scheme [claimed more than 600,000 acceptance locations and more than 19 million application inclusions, and was added to a handset maker's wallet](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/samsung-sa-expands-digital-wallet-ecosystem/xA9POvNE6n8qo4J8). The figures are the provider's own. Nothing on this ledger relates it to the bank-led national payment code standard, still at an eight-member test cohort.</details> | Advanced |
| Sudan | The [national payment switch went live](https://allafrica.com/stories/202608100282.html) in August 2026 with one bank connected, and a wallet [added recurring debits](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/zakhirwallet_zakher-digitalpayments-fintech-activity-7497610519567056897-U0ps) two weeks later. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The central bank's payments arm launched live switch services on 10 August 2026, connecting the first of the country's banks and completing a first cash withdrawal through it, with the remainder to be phased in ([launch](https://allafrica.com/stories/202608100282.html)).<br><br>On 24 August a mobile wallet launched an interface for pre-authorised recurring deductions, offered as an alternative to cheques for subscriptions, instalments, rent, government fees and business collections ([interface](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/zakhirwallet_zakher-digitalpayments-fintech-activity-7497610519567056897-U0ps)).<br><br>One bank connected is a switch that is live rather than a switch that is national; the dominant mobile-money service remains dominant by default.</details> | Advanced |
| Tanzania | [Instant payment value nearly doubled](https://www.thecitizen.co.tz/tanzania/business/tanzania-steps-up-cash-lite-drive-as-digital-payments-hit-new-highs-5541316) year on year on calendar-2025 volumes reported to Parliament, and a [US$28m mobile money core upgrade](https://www.tanzaniainvest.com/telecoms/vodacom-new-mpesa-fintech-launch) added interoperability with a global payment provider. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The throughput figures were reported in the 2026/27 budget; a third figure on a different basis is held and cannot be charted with these ([throughput](https://www.thecitizen.co.tz/tanzania/business/tanzania-steps-up-cash-lite-drive-as-digital-payments-hit-new-highs-5541316), [platform](https://www.bot.go.tz/Publications/Other/Working%20Papers%20Series/en/2025051609501183.pdf)).<br><br>The core upgrade launched in 2026 with wallet interoperability to a global payment provider added in May ([upgrade](https://www.tanzaniainvest.com/telecoms/vodacom-new-mpesa-fintech-launch), [alliance](https://devtelecoms.com/telecom-technology/financial-services/20298-vodacom-m-pesa-tanzania-announces-alliance-with-paypal.html)).<br><br>A school-fee payment service connected more than 800 schools ([schools](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/21/airtel-money-school-pay-expands-digital-fee-payments-to-800-schools-in-tanzania/)), a wallet-based investment platform recorded more than TZS 370bn in customer investments to March 2026 on the operator's own results ([investing](https://www.telemediamagazine.com/vodacom-lesotho-brings-micro-investing-to-m-pesa-as-africas-mobile-wallets-move-into-wealth/)), and a government payments programme was described by a ministry official as still to be created ([programme](https://www.mawasiliano.go.tz/news/mfumo-wa-jamii-x-change-kuunganisha-mifumo-ya-tehama-ya-kisekta)).<br><br>2026-08-30 — the state operator and the Zanzibar Football Federation [signed a sponsorship and partnership agreement to introduce stadium e-ticketing on the operator's N-Card platform](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/31/ttcl-and-zff-launch-digital-push-with-n-card-e-ticketing-system/). No launch date, ticket volume or fee is stated, and the base holds nothing on the platform's other uses or acceptance footprint.</details> | Advanced |
| Togo | [Six institutions](https://www.togofirst.com/fr/finance/0603-18394-bceao-six-acteurs-autorises-a-ouvrir-au-public-les-services-de-paiement-instantane-au-togo) are on the central bank's list authorised to open instant-payment services to the public, and the [mandatory connection deadline](https://lefaso.net/spip.php?article147670) moved from June to September 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Integrated, operational, committed and authorised to open to the public are four different claims across four accounts, and the counts are not one series ([authorised](https://www.togofirst.com/fr/finance/0603-18394-bceao-six-acteurs-autorises-a-ouvrir-au-public-les-services-de-paiement-instantane-au-togo), [institutions](https://www.togofirst.com/fr/finance/0408-16870-togo-7-institutions-financieres-sur-la-plateforme-de-paiements-instantanes-de-la-bceao), [launch](https://www.togofirst.com/en/banking/0110-17197-bceao-launches-instant-payments-platform-in-togo-with-three-banks), [sandbox](https://www.togofirst.com/en/itc/1811-17593-bceao-launches-developer-sandbox-for-instant-payments), [platform](https://www.togofirst.com/fr/finance/1404-18708-mixx-togo-lance-les-paiements-et-virements-instantanes-dans-luemoa-via-la-plateforme-pi-spi)).<br><br>The deadline moved to 30 September 2026 for banks, electronic-money issuers and payment institutions and to 30 June 2027 for microfinance institutions; the extension source is union-wide and binds domestic institutions by construction, with no national report of it held ([extension](https://lefaso.net/spip.php?article147670), [original](https://www.togofirst.com/fr/finance/0304-18641-la-bceao-fixe-au-30-juin-l-adhesion-des-operateurs-a-sa-plateforme-de-paiement-instantane-pi-spi)).<br><br>Four incompatible descriptions of participation and a slipped deadline are what the record supports; a single participation figure would be manufactured.</details> | Mixed, six institutions authorised on the regional instant platform while the mandatory connection deadline slipped by three months |
| Zambia | Mobile wallets [reached a bank's point-of-sale terminals and cash machines](https://www.zanaco.co.zm/2026/03/04/momo-payments-on-zanaco-pos-launch/), and [councils were onboarded for electronic fee payment](https://www.lusakatimes.com/2026/07/10/e-council-services-enhance-efficiency-at-solwezi-municipal-council/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The National Payment Systems Act of 2007 established regulatory authority over the payment mechanisms underlying these rails](https://www.boz.zm/national-payment-systems-act.htm).<br><br>2026-03 — [a mobile money wallet gained interoperability with a commercial bank's point-of-sale terminals and cash machines](https://www.zanaco.co.zm/2026/03/04/momo-payments-on-zanaco-pos-launch/), a concrete person-to-business and cash-out expansion.<br><br>2026-07 — [local authorities including one municipal council were onboarded to the government service bus, enabling electronic payment of council fees and levies](https://www.lusakatimes.com/2026/07/10/e-council-services-enhance-efficiency-at-solwezi-municipal-council/). Person-to-government payment reaching local rather than central government is the newer half, and no count of councils, transactions or value is published against it.</details> | Advanced |
| Zimbabwe | The [instant payment interchange](https://africanenda.org/wp-content/uploads/siips_2024_ZIPIT_CaseStudy_en.pdf) has run cross-domain transfers since 2011 across 17 commercial banks, and [interoperable code payments](https://www.zbcnews.co.zw/electronic-transactions-top-zig1-trillion-as-zimbabwe-deepens-digital-finance/) are in rollout on the switch operator's report. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The interchange was established in 2011 by the payments association and the central bank against cash shortages, when non-cash payment ran on cash machines, terminals and cheques that took up to seven days to settle and mobile money did not yet exist in the country; it carries real-time credit-push transfers between bank accounts and mobile wallets across 17 commercial banks, person-to-person transfer remaining its most used case ([case study](https://africanenda.org/wp-content/uploads/siips_2024_ZIPIT_CaseStudy_en.pdf)).<br><br>The code-payment rollout is company-reported ([codes](https://www.zbcnews.co.zw/electronic-transactions-top-zig1-trillion-as-zimbabwe-deepens-digital-finance/), [transactions](https://bullszimbabwe.com/digital-payments-surge-but-adoption-lags-survey/)).<br><br>Wallet cash-out onto bank machine estates has no launch dates in the source, so the analysis date is used ([cash-out](https://www.equityaxis.net/post/19212/2026/7/the-o-mari-effect-zimbabwe-s-mobile-money-war-goes-physical-as-netone-remains-idle)). Ten cash-recycler machines are installed at retail outlets with 78 stated in the pipeline; the figures are the reporting outlet's rather than company-audited ([recyclers](https://www.equityaxis.net/post/19212/2026/7/the-o-mari-effect-zimbabwe-s-mobile-money-war-goes-physical-as-netone-remains-idle)).</details> | Advanced |

## Population uptake

| Country | Developments | Progress |
|---|---|---|
| Algeria | [Over 22m cards and 104,000 terminals were in issue at end-March 2026](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/algeria-s-electronic-payment-market-surpasses-22-million-cards), with payments up 46% in 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Fewer than a tenth of merchants operated a terminal at the window's opening. At end-March 2026 there were [over 22m cards including nearly 18m of the postal operator's, over 104,000 terminals and 4,713 cash machines](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/algeria-s-electronic-payment-market-surpasses-22-million-cards), with [electronic payments of DZD 939bn in 2025, up 46% year on year, and fewer than a tenth of merchants still operating a terminal](https://www.echoroukonline.com/paiement-electronique-voici-les-derniers-chiffres).<br><br>2026-06-30 - [more than 1,000 web merchants were connected, passing that threshold, alongside reported growth in online and mobile payment and in acceptance at physical shops over the first half of 2026](https://alger16.dz/?p=65795). The half-year indicators are reported in narrative form without a published series.</details> | Advanced |
| Angola | Financial inclusion [stood at 51.7% in the first quarter of 2026 against a 65% target for 2027](https://angop.ao/noticias/economia/governo-anuncia-nova-etapa-na-estrategia-nacional-de-inclusao-financeira/), with banking at 32% and digital payments up about 56%. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-07-09 — a state minister's scorecard [puts financial inclusion at 51.7% at the first quarter of 2026 against a 65% target for 2027, banking at 32% or 5.7m people, and mobile and digital payments up about 56% year on year](https://angop.ao/noticias/economia/governo-anuncia-nova-etapa-na-estrategia-nacional-de-inclusao-financeira/). The strategy behind the figures launched in December 2025 and no pre-strategy baseline is held, so the target is measurable and the movement toward it is not.<br><br>The bancarisation figure has no series behind it either: [a 2023 industry account put roughly half the population unbanked](https://www.gsma.com/solutions-and-impact/connectivity-for-good/mobile-for-development/programme/mobile-money/mobile-money-in-angola-how-afrimoney-is-paving-the-way/) on a different base, and the two cannot be read as a trend.<br><br>The next phase is stated as letting mobile and digital payment providers extend credit, which would move the same population from payments into lending with no consumer-protection instrument on this ledger behind it.</details> | Advanced |
| Benin | Mobile money [reached 88.09% penetration on 11.65m active accounts](https://www.lanation.bj/numerique/communications-electroniques-lessor-des-usages-numeriques-transforme-le-marche-beninois), one operator holding about 51% of accounts and 83% of revenue. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-05-19 — the regulator's 2025 report [puts mobile money at 88.09% penetration on 11.65m active accounts at end-2025, with one operator holding about 51% of accounts and about 83% of revenue](https://www.lanation.bj/numerique/communications-electroniques-lessor-des-usages-numeriques-transforme-le-marche-beninois), against [248.7m electronic-money accounts across the union in 2024](https://www.lanation.bj/economie/finance-numerique-et-inclusion-plus-de-248-millions-de-comptes-momo-dans-luemoa).<br><br>The revenue share is the finding. A market where half the accounts produce four fifths of the revenue is not a competitive one, and nothing on this ledger records a regulatory response, an interconnection remedy or a price intervention.<br><br>Uptake at this level makes mobile money the effective account layer for the population, which is what the cash levy and the toll and transfer programmes reported elsewhere all assume.</details> | Advanced, in accounts, with concentration unchanged |
| Botswana | A remittance wallet [gained a card for cash withdrawal and in-store payment](https://www.connectingafrica.com/fintech/mukuru-launches-mobile-wallet-linked-visa-card-in-botswana), and [a bank and a taxi association launched card acceptance with loans conditioned on the banking history it builds](https://businessweekly.co.bw/companies-markets/absa-opens-new-financing-cashless-payment-opportunities-for-taxi-operators). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-08-18 — [a card issued against a remittance wallet balance added cash withdrawal, in-store payment and domestic online payment to a wallet that previously carried transfer and cash-out only, with no separate bank account required, working across every mobile network, each transaction protected by a code and a lost card blockable from the handset](https://www.connectingafrica.com/fintech/mukuru-launches-mobile-wallet-linked-visa-card-in-botswana), [launched the day before](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/18/mukuru-launches-card-in-botswana-with-access-bank-and-visa/). [The operator puts the gap it addresses at 38 per cent of adults unbanked against mobile penetration of 164 per cent](https://www.connectingafrica.com/fintech/mukuru-launches-mobile-wallet-linked-visa-card-in-botswana).<br><br>2026-08-09 — [a commercial bank and a taxi association launched card acceptance on the bank's tap product for taxi operators, with access to business loans of up to P60,000 conditioned on a formal banking history built through it](https://businessweekly.co.bw/companies-markets/absa-opens-new-financing-cashless-payment-opportunities-for-taxi-operators). It is a single bank's product with a single association, no operator count or fee schedule is stated, and the loan access is tied to banking with the same institution.<br><br>Both arrived in the last three weeks of the window and both route the unbanked into the formal system through a private product rather than a public rail.</details> | Advanced |
| Burkina Faso | The country [holds 9.53% of the union's 248 million electronic-money accounts, fourth of eight states](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2026-03/Rapport%20annuel%20sur%20les%20services%20financiers%20num%C3%A9riques%20dans%20l%27UEMOA%20-%202024.pdf), and [a local application aggregating all three operators passed 400,000 downloads](https://lefaso.net/spip.php?article145328). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-01-01 — the regional central bank's 2024 report [puts the country at 9.53% of the union-wide 248 million electronic-money accounts opened at end-2024, fourth of the eight member states, with the national treasury one of only two treasuries among the 69 issuing initiatives](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2026-03/Rapport%20annuel%20sur%20les%20services%20financiers%20num%C3%A9riques%20dans%20l%27UEMOA%20-%202024.pdf). The report publishes the country share rather than a national account total, so the level has to be inferred and the base does not infer it.<br><br>2026-03-27 — [a locally built application aggregating all three operators and five mobile-money wallets reports more than 400,000 downloads and a high store rating](https://lefaso.net/spip.php?article145328), on the developer's own figures in a promotional account.<br><br>A treasury issuing electronic money is the distinctive feature here: the state is a participant in the payments market it also taxes through, reported under revenue collection.</details> | Advanced |
| Burundi | Cash [held about 45% of digital-payment value, with about 1.6m of 7.6m mobile wallets active](https://www.burunditimes.com/burundi-digital-payments-see-growth-but-cash-still-dominates/) in the last reading the base holds. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-09-29 — central bank data for the second quarter of 2025 [put deposits and withdrawals at about 45% of digital-payment value and about 1.6m of 7.6m mobile wallets active](https://www.burunditimes.com/burundi-digital-payments-see-growth-but-cash-still-dominates/). No baseline preceded it and no reading has followed it.<br><br>The dormancy rate is the finding: roughly four in five wallets opened are not in use, which bounds what the instant payment system reported above can reach. [Its own user figure of about 224,000](https://www.jimberemag.org/une-revolution-de-leconomie-digitale-au-burundi/) sits against those registered wallets.<br><br>No quarterly series, no account-ownership survey and no financial-inclusion strategy appears anywhere on this ledger.</details> | No change |
| Cameroon | The regional switch carried [20.3m transactions worth FCFA 863bn in 2025, up 40% by volume](https://leconomie.info/article/paiements-numeriques-gimacpay-enregistre-203-millions-de-transactions-pour-863-milliards-de-fcfa-en-2025), across 153 participants. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The switch carried [about 14.5m transactions worth about FCFA 710bn in 2024](https://lejour.cm/mobile-money-gimacpay-franchit-la-barre-des-600-milliards-fcfa-de-transactions/), and [20.3m transactions worth FCFA 863bn in 2025, up 40% by volume, across 153 participants, with 84.33m transactions worth FCFA 3,624bn cumulative since service start](https://leconomie.info/article/paiements-numeriques-gimacpay-enregistre-203-millions-de-transactions-pour-863-milliards-de-fcfa-en-2025).<br><br>The figures are regional, not Cameroonian. The nearest national measure is the regulator's count of [over 11m active mobile financial services subscribers in 2024](https://techafricanews.com/2025/12/09/cameroons-telecom-sector-grows-18-in-2024-driven-by-mobile-and-digital-services/), with no comparable prior-year figure held, so national uptake is a level rather than a movement.</details> | Advanced |
| Cape Verde | [380,545 cards and 13,249 terminals](https://www.bcv.cv/SiteCollectionDocuments/2025/Relat%C3%B3rio%20de%20Sistema%20de%20Pagamentos%202024.pdf) against 232 cash machines, on the central bank's own 2024 report. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [payments report for 2024](https://www.bcv.cv/SiteCollectionDocuments/2025/Relat%C3%B3rio%20de%20Sistema%20de%20Pagamentos%202024.pdf) is the base's first consolidated coverage figure for Cabo Verde: 380,545 cards, 13,249 point-of-sale terminals and 232 cash machines, with 132,405 daily operations worth CVE 553.8m.<br><br>It also records an instant transfer pilot under way, which is the next rail rather than a measure of use. No earlier comparable figure is held, so the direction of travel cannot be stated.</details> | No change, a first published coverage count |
| Central African Republic | The country [held 0.43% of regional payment-operation volume in 2024, the smallest but one](https://www.beac.int/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/RAPPORT-SUR-LES-SERVICES-DE-PAIEMENT-DANS-LA-CEMAC-2024-.pdf), and no national account count or value figure is published. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-04-08 — [the regional central bank's 2024 payment-services report puts the country at 0.43% of regional payment-operation volume, the smallest but one, against the largest member state's 65.10%](https://www.beac.int/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/RAPPORT-SUR-LES-SERVICES-DE-PAIEMENT-DANS-LA-CEMAC-2024-.pdf). The report holds no value figure or account count for the country.<br><br>That share is the only measure of payment uptake on this ledger. No wallet count, transaction total, agent network figure or account-ownership survey appears at any date, and the regulator publishes nothing.<br><br>The uses that do exist are narrow and state-driven: salaries for officials outside the banking network and, since August 2026, electricity bills — both reported above, both dependent on one operator.</details> | No change |
| Chad | A [second mobile-money operator obtained a payment-institution licence](https://leconomie.info/tchad-moov-money-rejoint-le-club-restreint-des-etablissements-de-paiement-agrees/), joining one [licensed in 2023](https://leconomie.info/tchad-moov-money-rejoint-le-club-restreint-des-etablissements-de-paiement-agrees/) and a [multichannel wallet licensed in 2025](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/solutions/tchad-konoom-elargit-lacces-aux-paiements-numeriques-grace-a-ses-plateformes-web-et-mobiles). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The second operator's approval is dated April 2026 on its own filing and July 2026 in the trade press — two dates for one licence ([licence](https://leconomie.info/tchad-moov-money-rejoint-le-club-restreint-des-etablissements-de-paiement-agrees/), [filing](https://www.iam.ma/documents/66341/0/Maroc+Telecom+-+Rapport+financier+S1+2026+%282%29.pdf/261de29d-cee8-407f-1e28-dbb3a53f985f)).<br><br>The first operator was licensed as a payment institution in 2023 at year precision, and its launch date is not established ([first](https://leconomie.info/tchad-moov-money-rejoint-le-club-restreint-des-etablissements-de-paiement-agrees/), [launch](https://communicationsafrica.com/mobile/airtel-launched-mobile-money-transfers-in-chad)). A multichannel wallet is in service, licensed in 2025, with no user, agent-network or transaction figure published ([wallet](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/solutions/tchad-konoom-elargit-lacces-aux-paiements-numeriques-grace-a-ses-plateformes-web-et-mobiles)).<br><br>Mobile-money transaction value is held from a central bank measurement for end-2024 ([value](https://leconomie.info/tchad-moov-money-rejoint-le-club-restreint-des-etablissements-de-paiement-agrees/)), which is the only quantity in the payments record and is nearly two years old.</details> | Advanced |
| Comoros | The central bank's 2024 report records [669,584 electronic-money accounts opened, 29.1 per cent active](https://www.mayottehebdo.com/actualite/economie/comores-lancement-de-nouveaux-systemes-de-paiement/), and a 39 per cent banking rate. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Relayed from the central bank's own 2024 annual report: [669,584 accounts opened of which 29.1 per cent active, an electronic-money float of KMF 2,700,000,000, and nine banks and financial institutions with 175 branches, twenty-six cash machines and 140 decentralised finance branches](https://www.mayottehebdo.com/actualite/economie/comores-lancement-de-nouveaux-systemes-de-paiement/), and a [national banking rate of 39 per cent against a projection of 70 per cent by 2030](https://www.mayottehebdo.com/actualite/economie/comores-lancement-de-nouveaux-systemes-de-paiement/).<br><br>The report itself is not held and the figures reach the base second-hand. No earlier dated figure exists, so no direction can be stated, and an earlier banking-rate target for end-2025 is recorded and never measured against.</details> | Advanced |
| Cote d'Ivoire | [99,649,204 electronic-money accounts were open at end-2024, 40.07% of the monetary union's total](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2026-03/Rapport%20annuel%20sur%20les%20services%20financiers%20num%C3%A9riques%20dans%20l%27UEMOA%20-%202024.pdf), of which 26.46% active. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The base's earlier position was the [regulator's last published market statistics: 13,441,990 mobile-money subscribers moving FCFA 8,236bn a year on 2018 data](http://itu.int/en/ITU-D/Regional-Presence/Africa/Documents/ICT%20Indicators%202019/Presentations/session-4-cote-divoire-panorama-des-telecoms-tic-fr.pdf).<br><br>The central bank's annual report puts the country at [99,649,204 electronic-money accounts opened at 31 December 2024, 40.07% of the monetary union's total and the largest share in it, of which 26,370,627 active at 26.46%](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2026-03/Rapport%20annuel%20sur%20les%20services%20financiers%20num%C3%A9riques%20dans%20l%27UEMOA%20-%202024.pdf). The same report records [2,549,268,326 transactions, 21.4% of the union's volume and 34.7% of its value, and merchant acceptance points up from 1,131,590 to 2,719,463 in the year](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2026-03/Rapport%20annuel%20sur%20les%20services%20financiers%20num%C3%A9riques%20dans%20l%27UEMOA%20-%202024.pdf).<br><br>The two ends are on different definitions - mobile-money subscribers against electronic-money accounts opened - so the direction is the union comparison, not the arithmetic between them.</details> | Advanced |
| Djibouti | A [memorandum for a national digital wallet was signed on 16 September 2025](https://idtechwire.com/djibouti-partners-with-visa-to-develop-national-digital-wallet), targeting a 60% cut in cash use. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Nothing existed at the window's opening. 2025-09-16 - a [memorandum was signed with a card network and a biometrics supplier for a national digital wallet, with nothing in service and no rollout calendar](https://idtechwire.com/djibouti-partners-with-visa-to-develop-national-digital-wallet).<br><br>The stated target of a large cut in cash use within three years is the parties' own. No baseline cash-use figure, account count or transaction volume exists anywhere on this ledger to measure it against, and the wallet already in service publishes none either - so the country's payment uptake is stated only as an ambition.</details> | Advanced |
| DR Congo | The most precisely dated figure is [29m accounts at 34.6% penetration](https://www.connectingafrica.com/financial-inclusion/how-onafriq-is-growing-digital-finance-amid-uncertainty-in-drc); the base holds 24m, 29m, 30m and 35m. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The only dated, attributable count is [29m accounts and 34.6% penetration, from a payments aggregator's fourth-quarter 2024 figures](https://www.connectingafrica.com/financial-inclusion/how-onafriq-is-growing-digital-finance-amid-uncertainty-in-drc). An [operator cites about 35m users in April 2026](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/4723/vodacom-congo-calls-for-integrated-financial-ecosystem-to-enhance-inclusion-in-drc), and the base holds 24m, 29m, 30m and 35m across sources.<br><br>The divergence is the finding: four counts, none reconcilable, and no published central-bank series. The nearest official figure is [about 58% financial inclusion against 25 to 30% bank-account penetration, the gap attributed to mobile-money use without a bank account](https://actualite.cd/2026/05/04/inclusion-financiere-en-rdc-58-dacces-contre-25-30-de-bancarisation-la-bcc-parie-sur-la), with no earlier dated rate held.</details> | No change, four counts of mobile-money accounts circulate and none is reconcilable to another |
| Egypt | The instant payment network reached [16m users and EGP 4.2tn transacted to June 2025](https://gate.ahram.org.eg/News/5541984.aspx), alongside [over 55.5m wallet accounts](https://www.elwatannews.com/news/details/8325979). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The network had [12.5m users and about 1.5bn transactions worth up to EGP 2.9tn in calendar 2024, domestic transfers only](https://www.cbe.org.eg/ar/news-publications/news/2024/12/30/12/31/cbe-extending-the-exemption-of-individuals-from-instapay-for-a-renewable-period-of-3-months). It now has [16m users and EGP 4.2tn transacted from July 2024 to June 2025, with inbound remittances credited instantly at all banks operating in the country](https://gate.ahram.org.eg/News/5541984.aspx). No figure fresher than end-June 2025 is held.<br><br>Wallets stand at [over 55.5m accounts transacting about EGP 1.8tn to June 2025, growth telco-led through the three operators' products](https://www.elwatannews.com/news/details/8325979); a Senate proposal of June 2026 puts the base at 45m against about 6m bank cards, an unreconciled framing of the same stock.<br><br>Cards and acceptance stand at [29.1m debit cards at about 40% of the market, 34.3m prepaid, about 7m credit, over 26,000 cash machines and about 275,000 terminals, with national scheme cards still stated at 43.5m from end-2024](https://gate.ahram.org.eg/News/5335311.aspx). About [16.7 million adults had no formal access to credit at June 2025](https://techcabal.com/2026/06/08/egypts-blnk-raises-37-million-to-expand-lending/).</details> | Advanced |
| Equatorial Guinea | Two wallets launched in 2026, on a base holding [0.41 per cent of regional payment volume](https://www.beac.int/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/RAPPORT-SUR-LES-SERVICES-DE-PAIEMENT-DANS-LA-CEMAC-2024-.pdf), the smallest share of six member states. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The share is measured at end-2024 by the regional central bank ([regional report](https://www.beac.int/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/RAPPORT-SUR-LES-SERVICES-DE-PAIEMENT-DANS-LA-CEMAC-2024-.pdf)).<br><br>A bank wallet launched in February 2026 with a payment service and a prepaid card, with no user or transaction figure published ([bank wallet](https://realequatorialguinea.com/economia/bange-lanza-bange-mobile-ge-la-nueva-app-que-facilita-pagos-y-servicios-financieros-en-guinea-ecuatorial/)).<br><br>The state operator named a wallet of its own as part of its modernisation, again with no user or transaction figure ([operator wallet](https://realequatorialguinea.com/destacado/politica/getesa-planea-su-modernizacion-5g-y-wi-fi-publico-en-todo-el-territorio-de-guinea-ecuatorial/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Eswatini | [Account ownership](https://www.times.co.sz/business/readmore.php?bhsadjgfoh=Mobile+money+drives+financial+inclusion+growth&bvhdgsj=Business+and+Economy&yiphi=435) rose on a global survey cited in the record, against a [cash share of person-to-business payments](https://www.biometricupdate.com/202508/robust-dpi-inevitable-for-eswatinis-economic-jobs-boom-world-bank) the lender assessed as high. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The account ownership figure is a reference-study figure, cited rather than absorbed ([ownership](https://www.times.co.sz/business/readmore.php?bhsadjgfoh=Mobile+money+drives+financial+inclusion+growth&bvhdgsj=Business+and+Economy&yiphi=435)), and the cash share comes from a lender assessment of digital public infrastructure ([cash](https://www.biometricupdate.com/202508/robust-dpi-inevitable-for-eswatinis-economic-jobs-boom-world-bank)).<br><br>The mobile money platform migrated to a cloud-native vendor platform across four markets; the performance figures are the vendor's own and no downtime, cost or subscriber-impact figure is stated ([migration](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/21/ericsson-mtn-complete-momo-cloud-migration-across-four-african-markets/)).<br><br>Neither uptake figure is a domestic series, so what the payment switch and the post-office channel changed cannot be measured from this base.</details> | Advanced |
| Ethiopia | The incumbent's wallet reports [60.6m users and 4.19tn birr transacted](https://www.fanamc.com/english/215-8-billion-birr-revenue-and-9-13-trillion-birr-in-digital-transactions-ethio-telecoms-new-economic-footprint/) in the year to June 2026, against 2.58m monthly active users on the challenger. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The incumbent's wallet moved 4.19tn birr in the year to June 2026, about 11bn birr a day, across 60.6m users, 410,700 agents and 440,100 merchants, with 21.64m customers on digital credit and microfinance ([wallet results](https://www.fanamc.com/english/215-8-billion-birr-revenue-and-9-13-trillion-birr-in-digital-transactions-ethio-telecoms-new-economic-footprint/)).<br><br>The challenger reports monthly active users doubled to 2.58m in the quarter to June 2026 and registered merchants up 147.7% to 89,877, while mobile money is about 2% of the unit's service revenue against 45.6% in Kenya ([quarterly results](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-finances/0308-57937-safaricom-s-ethiopian-unit-delivers-rapid-growth-but-mobile-money-struggles)); the base also holds 5.2m active users for the quarter to December 2025, and the two counts are not reconciled ([earlier count](https://itweb.africa/article/m-pesa-ethiopia-surges-258-as-ethswitch-fuels-growth/)).<br><br>The dominant state bank reports 3.48 billion digital transactions worth more than 22 trillion birr in 2025/26, which it puts at about 70 per cent of national digital transaction value ([bank figures](https://capitalethiopia.com/2026/08/16/ai-driven-cyber-threats-raise-alarm-for-ethiopias-financial-sector/)).<br><br>The Global Findex 2025 reading puts [account ownership at 49 per cent of adults in 2024, up three points from 46 per cent in 2021](https://digitalfinance.shega.co/insights/articles/findex-2025-and-ethiopia-s-digital-financial-leap-momentum-without-maturity), against more than 200 million digital accounts opened over the period, with financial literacy, limited smartphone access and physical distance named as what still excludes. Accounts opened and adults included move in opposite directions and nothing reconciles the two.</details> | Advanced |
| Gabon | [4.5m mobile-money accounts of which 1.6m active](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1112-51310-gabon-speeds-up-digital-payments-rollout-with-madigipaie-program) at end-2024 remain the base's current statement, against 457,377 cardholders. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The ministry's end-2024 figures were cited at a payment launch in December 2025 and nothing later has been published: 368.3m transactions worth FCFA 4,087bn in 2024, against FCFA 3,487bn in 2023 ([accounts](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1112-51310-gabon-speeds-up-digital-payments-rollout-with-madigipaie-program)).<br><br>On cards, the bank association reports 457,377 cardholders among 673,793 bank clients at end-June 2026, against 326 cash machines and 1,535 merchant terminals ([card estate](https://leconomie.info/article/gabon-les-banques-comptent-457-377-cartes-bancaires-pour-seulement-246-de-paiements-sur-tpe)). Of 1,101,434 transactions in June, 62.41 per cent were cash withdrawals and 24.61 per cent terminal payments ([the same return](https://leconomie.info/article/gabon-les-banques-comptent-457-377-cartes-bancaires-pour-seulement-246-de-paiements-sur-tpe)).<br><br>A terminal estate 4.7 times the size of the cash-machine estate carries under half its traffic, on industry-reported figures.</details> | No change |
| Gambia | [2.9m active users against 5.4m registered accounts](https://www.cbg.gm/downloads-file/232cd73c-132c-11f1-8725-02e599c15748) in February 2026; the bank's own later figures disagree. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [February 2026 release](https://www.cbg.gm/downloads-file/232cd73c-132c-11f1-8725-02e599c15748) puts active mobile money users at 2.9m, 53 per cent of 5.4m registered accounts — a register larger than the population, so it counts accounts rather than people.<br><br>The bank then contradicted itself: its [May 2026 minutes put registered fintech users at 4.6m](https://www.cbg.gm/downloads-file/128680cd-9cbc-11f1-8725-02e599c15748) for the same March quarter its [May release gave as 5.4m](https://www.cbg.gm/downloads-file/814b3831-5532-11f1-8725-02e599c15748), and the [August 2026 release dropped the count altogether](https://www.cbg.gm/downloads-file/7a57e5cf-9cba-11f1-8725-02e599c15748) while publishing quarterly cash-in of D28.2bn against cash-out of D32.1bn. The divergence stands unresolved.</details> | Mixed, use is rising while the register itself is disputed |
| Ghana | [84.6m registered wallets and 26.4m active](https://www.myjoyonline.com/mobile-money-transactions-hit-gh%c2%a2492-9bn-in-june/) at 90 days in June 2026, with float at a record GH¢40bn from GH¢28.9bn. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Registered wallets rose from 76.4m in June 2025, and there are one million registered agents of whom 546,000 are active ([central bank data](https://www.myjoyonline.com/mobile-money-transactions-hit-gh%c2%a2492-9bn-in-june/)); the inclusion figure is the 26.4m active, under a third of registered wallets ([central bank data](https://www.myjoyonline.com/mobile-money-transactions-hit-gh%c2%a2492-9bn-in-june/)).<br><br>The dominant operator reports 19.3m active users at 31 December 2025 against 18.3m at 30 June 2026, with neither source stating the definition, so the direction cannot be read against the national series ([operator accounts](https://mtn.com.gh/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Scancom-PLC-MTN-Ghana-H1-2026-Results.pdf)).<br><br>[The dominant operator's chief executive put daily transactions at over 26 million while stating that fewer than 0.3% of the user base is actively investing and only a small proportion saves regularly](https://www.itweb.africa/article/mtn-pushes-mobile-money-beyond-payments/PmxVEMKEYaAvQY85). The gap between volume and depth is the operator's own framing, given in an interview and not tested against the central bank's series.</details> | Advanced |
| Guinea | [Account access rose from 4% of adults in 2011 to close to 36% in 2024 on the central bank governor's account](https://guineenews.org/2026/07/22/guinee-la-bcrg-lance-nimbapay-la-plateforme-nationale-qui-revolutionne-les-paiements-instantanes-et-linteroperabilite/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[At the NimbaPay launch on 22 July 2026 the central bank governor put access to financial accounts at 4% in 2011 and close to 36% in 2024, attributing the rise to electronic money](https://guineenews.org/2026/07/22/guinee-la-bcrg-lance-nimbapay-la-plateforme-nationale-qui-revolutionne-les-paiements-instantanes-et-linteroperabilite/).<br><br>The figure is the governor's own, given in a launch speech; the base holds no survey series behind it and no breakdown by sex, region or income.</details> | Advanced |
