| Guinea-Bissau | Active electronic-money accounts [grew 33.28% in 2024, the union's second-fastest rate](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2026-03/Rapport%20annuel%20sur%20les%20services%20financiers%20num%C3%A9riques%20dans%20l%27UEMOA%20-%202024.pdf), on a regional report published in January 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-01-01 — the regional central bank's annual report on digital financial services [records Guinea-Bissau's active electronic-money accounts up 33.28% in 2024, the union's second-highest growth rate after Togo, on a 1.94% share of accounts opened union-wide and about 1% of regional transaction value, served by two electronic-money issuing initiatives of the 69 operating across the union](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2026-03/Rapport%20annuel%20sur%20les%20services%20financiers%20num%C3%A9riques%20dans%20l%27UEMOA%20-%202024.pdf).<br><br>The data year is 2024 and the publication falls inside the window, so what the window holds is the arrival of the figure rather than the growth itself. These are shares within a regional report and not a national release: the base holds no account total, no transaction count and no payments statistic published in Guinea-Bissau.<br><br>No named mobile-money service, no interoperable switch and no consumer-protection rule for payments appears anywhere on this ledger.</details> | Advanced, on the regional regulator's 2024 figures |
| Kenya | [94.09m registered mobile-money accounts and 564,330 active agents](https://www.centralbank.go.ke/national-payments-system/mobile-payments/) in May 2026, with the dominant service on 40.99m 30-day-active customers. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Registered accounts rose from 87.46m and agents from 451,157 in August 2025, with agent cash movement of KSh 681.45bn in the month ([central bank release](https://www.centralbank.go.ke/national-payments-system/mobile-payments/)).<br><br>The dominant service reported KES 182.7bn revenue, KES 41.68tn transacted and 89.1 per cent market share in the year to March 2026, from 35.82m active customers and about 98 per cent share a year earlier ([results](https://www.safaricom.co.ke/images/Downloads/FY26-Earnings-Booklet-2026-7th-May-2026.pdf)); the central bank classified it systemically critical in January 2026 ([results](https://www.safaricom.co.ke/images/Downloads/FY26-Earnings-Booklet-2026-7th-May-2026.pdf), [designation](https://techcabal.com/2026/01/28/kenya-central-bank-m-pesa-failure-economy-collapse/)).<br><br>Formal financial inclusion is put at 83.7 per cent of adults against 26.7 per cent in 2006, on operator disclosure rather than an independent survey ([inclusion](https://itweb.africa/article/safaricom-posts-record-dividend-payout/lwrKxq3Y1mX7mg1o)), and a record KSh 1.4tn was disbursed through the overdraft product with the repayment ratio flat year on year ([overdraft](https://nation.africa/kenya/business/kenyans-fuliza-record-sh1-4-trillion-loans-for-food-rent-and-fees-5452126)).</details> | Advanced |
| Lesotho | Mobile money was spun into a [separately constituted entity](https://itweb.africa/article/vodacom-lesotho-invests-40m-to-supercharge-connectivity/P3gQ2qGAJZZ7nRD1) with [14,000 agents](https://publiceyenews.com/2026/06/17/vodacom-lesotho-at-30-from-one-portable-antenna-to-a-digital-nation/), while the second wallet has been [unchanged since 2024](https://lestimes.com/?p=86904). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The mobile money business was spun off into VCL Financial Services on 13 March 2026 ([spin-off](https://itweb.africa/article/vodacom-lesotho-invests-40m-to-supercharge-connectivity/P3gQ2qGAJZZ7nRD1)) and reported 14,000 agents by June 2026 ([anniversary](https://publiceyenews.com/2026/06/17/vodacom-lesotho-at-30-from-one-portable-antenna-to-a-digital-nation/)).<br><br>EcoCash completed a platform migration in October 2024 with group savings, payroll and fraud detection integrated, and wallet interest payouts stated as forthcoming; nothing later is held ([migration](https://lestimes.com/?p=86904)). No account or active-user figure is published for either.</details> | Advanced |
| Liberia | [52 per cent account ownership with more than 11m wallets](https://www.thenewdawnliberia.com/liberia-expands-digital-finance/) in 2024 is the base's current statement, with no later figure published. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The reading is two years old ([account ownership](https://www.thenewdawnliberia.com/liberia-expands-digital-finance/)).<br><br>It predates the instant payment system, the interoperable fee schedule and the movement of civil-servant salaries into wallets, so the one measure of uptake the base holds is from before everything that would have moved it.</details> | No change |
| Libya | Banking apps reached [4.30m subscribers](https://alwasat.ly/news/libya/510123) by February 2026 and electronic payment value reached [LD 643bn](https://libyaherald.com/2026/08/central-bank-source-to-libya-herald-cash-liquidity-to-reach-branches-sunday-withdrawal-limits-start-at-ld-3000-and-subject-to-increase) in the first seven months of 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Electronic payment value ran at LD 136bn in 2024, LD 389bn in 2025 and LD 643bn between 1 January and 31 July 2026 ([2026 to date](https://libyaherald.com/2026/08/central-bank-source-to-libya-herald-cash-liquidity-to-reach-branches-sunday-withdrawal-limits-start-at-ld-3000-and-subject-to-increase), [2025](https://libyaherald.com/2026/01/e-payment-transactions-for-2025-increased-by-186-percent-to-ld-389-billion-cbl)).<br><br>Banking-app subscribers stood at 4.30m in February 2026, and 200.7m transactions worth LD 313.6bn were recorded in 2025 ([subscribers](https://alwasat.ly/news/libya/510123), [2025 total](https://alwasat.ly/news/libya/509053)).<br><br>Part of that movement is scarcity rather than choice: branch cash withdrawals were capped at LD 3,000 a customer, and LD 4,000 in the south, from 9 August 2026 ([cash limits](https://libyaherald.com/2026/08/central-bank-source-to-libya-herald-cash-liquidity-to-reach-branches-sunday-withdrawal-limits-start-at-ld-3000-and-subject-to-increase)).</details> | Advanced |
| Madagascar | [Six to seven million active mobile money users](https://www.lexpress.mg/2026/08/alexandre-castel-directeur-general-de.html) are reported against a population of about thirty million, and the [largest platform](https://www.lexpress.mg/2026/08/alexandre-castel-directeur-general-de.html) migrated its core with outages. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The active-user range is the operator's figure for the whole market, and it is not comparable with a 73 per cent usage figure in a 2025 study that measured awareness and use rather than active accounts ([users](https://www.lexpress.mg/2026/08/alexandre-castel-directeur-general-de.html)).<br><br>The largest platform replaced its core in May 2026, with outages and balance-display faults through May and June that the operator states were regularised ([migration](https://www.lexpress.mg/2026/08/alexandre-castel-directeur-general-de.html)). The account is the operator's alone, given three months after the fact: no regulator is named, no incident report is published and no compensation is mentioned.</details> | Mixed, active users at about a fifth of the population while the largest platform's core migration disrupted service |

The central bank cautions that the subscription figure counts registered wallets.<br><br>Replacing worn banknotes cost MWK 50.6bn in 2025 and is expected to cost MWK 69.8bn in 2026, about 38 per cent higher ([banknotes](https://malawi24.com/2026/07/25/cash-habit-puts-pressure-on-malawis-currency-system/)); an economists' association president named government levies on digital money transfers as a reason cash use persists ([banknotes](https://malawi24.com/2026/07/25/cash-habit-puts-pressure-on-malawis-currency-system/)).</details> | Mixed, mobile money value up 94 per cent in a year while the cost of replacing worn banknotes rose 38 per cent |
| Mali | The country's [share of union electronic-money value fell to 11.7 per cent](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2026-03/Rapport%20annuel%20sur%20les%20services%20financiers%20num%C3%A9riques%20dans%20l%27UEMOA%20-%202024.pdf) in 2024, and its [distributor network fell 46.34 per cent](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2026-03/Rapport%20annuel%20sur%20les%20services%20financiers%20num%C3%A9riques%20dans%20l%27UEMOA%20-%202024.pdf), the steepest in the union. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Share of union transaction value fell from 12.1 per cent in 2023 to 11.7 per cent in 2024, and share of volume stood at 10.7 per cent, on volume growth of 18.58 per cent and 7.29 per cent of accounts opened ([shares](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2026-03/Rapport%20annuel%20sur%20les%20services%20financiers%20num%C3%A9riques%20dans%20l%27UEMOA%20-%202024.pdf)).<br><br>Distributor numbers fell 46.34 per cent in 2024, the steepest fall in the union ([network](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2026-03/Rapport%20annuel%20sur%20les%20services%20financiers%20num%C3%A9riques%20dans%20l%27UEMOA%20-%202024.pdf)). Volume rising while the agent network halves is the shape worth watching here: it is consistent with use concentrating on fewer, larger points rather than spreading.</details> | Mixed, transaction volume growing while the country's share of the union fell and the distributor network contracted sharply |

| Mozambique | Mobile money reached [1,313 accounts per thousand adults](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/atms-and-pos-terminals-lose-further-ground-to-mobile-money-in-mozambique/) in 2025 and [access points rose 36 per cent](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/atms-and-pos-terminals-lose-further-ground-to-mobile-money-in-mozambique/), while the [inclusion index held at 36.4](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/atms-and-pos-terminals-lose-further-ground-to-mobile-money-in-mozambique/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>Mobile money holdings rose from 1,093 to 1,313 accounts per thousand adults and bank accounts from 330 to 337 between 2024 and 2025, with women's mobile money holding rising from 924 to 1,144 ([accounts](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/atms-and-pos-terminals-lose-further-ground-to-mobile-money-in-mozambique/)).<br><br>Non-bank agents rose from 315,005 to 446,604 while cash machines fell from 1,391 to 1,383 and card terminals from 35,486 to 32,236, giving 482,359 access points in total, up 36 per cent ([access points](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/atms-and-pos-terminals-lose-further-ground-to-mobile-money-in-mozambique/)); the central bank attributes the terminal fall to decommissioning of inactive devices ([access points](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/atms-and-pos-terminals-lose-further-ground-to-mobile-money-in-mozambique/)).<br><br>The national inclusion index held at 36.4 points across the same year ([index](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/atms-and-pos-terminals-lose-further-ground-to-mobile-money-in-mozambique/)), against a strategy targeting 60 per cent of adults with a bank account by 2031 ([strategy](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/mozambique-e-money-accounts-have-grown-to-109-8-of-the-total-adult-population-government/)).</details> | Mixed, wallet accounts and agents growing sharply while card terminals fell and the inclusion index did not move |

| Nigeria | [Point-of-sale volumes fell 19.9 per cent](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/17/nigerians-moved-19-4-billion-through-atms/) as cash machine use rose, [operator wallet revenue rose about 132 per cent](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/25/mtn-wants-30-million-fintech-users/), and [financial inclusion stood at 74 per cent](https://thenationonlineng.net/payment-vision-2028-is-nigeria-ready-for-digital-economy/) against a 95 target. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Cash machine transactions rose 6.6 per cent year on year in the first quarter of 2026 with value up 64.6 per cent, while point-of-sale volumes fell 19.9 per cent to 2.92bn ([mix](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/17/nigerians-moved-19-4-billion-through-atms/)); agent-banking limits imposed in October 2025, single-provider terminals, a seventy-metre radius rule and a daily cap constrained the agent channel as the central bank pushed bank machine expansion, and the record carries no measure of what the limits did to access where there is no branch ([mix](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/17/nigerians-moved-19-4-billion-through-atms/)).<br><br>Operator wallet revenue grew about 132 per cent in the first half of 2026 with active wallets up 1.3m to 5m, against a medium-term target of 30 million ([wallets](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/25/mtn-wants-30-million-fintech-users/), [slump](https://techcabal.com/2026/07/31/mtns-airtime-lending-pause-drags-fintech-revenue/)).<br><br>Inclusion stood at 74 per cent in 2023 against a 95 per cent target for 2028 ([inclusion](https://thenationonlineng.net/payment-vision-2028-is-nigeria-ready-for-digital-economy/)), and retail investors traded ₦2.86tn of equities in five months, up 138.76 per cent ([investing](https://techcabal.com/2026/07/18/their-parents-lost-fortunes-theyre-buying-nigerian-stocks-anyway/)).<br><br>A digital bank passed 30 million registered users in Nigeria in September 2026, on its own count, which is not an active or transacting figure ([digital bank](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/breves/breves-simple/la-banque-numerique-fairmoney-depasse-les-30-millions-dusagers-au-nigeria)).</details> | Mixed, wallet revenue and retail investing rising while point-of-sale volumes fell a fifth under new agent-banking limits |
| Rwanda | [Active mobile money accounts](https://www.rura.rw/fileadmin/user_upload/RURA/Documents/Sectors/ICT/Statistics/Quarterly_publication/ICT_Sector_Statistics_Report_as_of_the_First_Quarter_2026.pdf) rose to the first quarter of 2026, while [accounts per agent](https://www.rura.rw/fileadmin/user_upload/RURA/Documents/Sectors/ICT/Statistics/Quarterly_publication/ICT_Sector_Statistics_Report_as_of_the_First_Quarter_2026.pdf) rose with the agent network lagging and the [second operator contracted](https://taarifa.rw/2026/07/31/ekash-was-designed-to-bridge-the-digital-divide-but-airtel-moneys-struggles-risk-leaving-rural-users-behind/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The account series comes from the regulator's quarterly statistics, and the earlier figure is derived from the growth rate rather than printed ([accounts](https://www.rura.rw/fileadmin/user_upload/RURA/Documents/Sectors/ICT/Statistics/Quarterly_publication/ICT_Sector_Statistics_Report_as_of_the_First_Quarter_2026.pdf), [rural](https://taarifa.rw/2026/07/31/ekash-was-designed-to-bridge-the-digital-divide-but-airtel-moneys-struggles-risk-leaving-rural-users-behind/)).<br><br>Accounts per agent rose because the agent network grew more slowly than the account base, and the earlier figures there are implied from growth rates rather than separately held ([agents](https://www.rura.rw/fileadmin/user_upload/RURA/Documents/Sectors/ICT/Statistics/Quarterly_publication/ICT_Sector_Statistics_Report_as_of_the_First_Quarter_2026.pdf)).<br><br>The second operator contracted in the cash-in and cash-out layer, which is precisely the layer the new payment rail does not touch ([operator](https://taarifa.rw/2026/07/31/ekash-was-designed-to-bridge-the-digital-divide-but-airtel-moneys-struggles-risk-leaving-rural-users-behind/)). Formal financial access was reported at the payment system's national launch, and a second source puts inclusion higher on a different metric ([access](https://african.business/2026/07/innov-africa-deals/rwanda-unifies-digital-payments-with-national-launch-of-ekash)).</details> | Mixed, active mobile money accounts and formal financial access both rising while the agent network lags and the second operator contracts |
| Senegal | The country's [share of regional mobile-money transaction volume](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2026-03/Rapport%20annuel%20sur%20les%20services%20financiers%20num%C3%A9riques%20dans%20l%27UEMOA%20-%202024.pdf) rose on the central bank's own 2024 series, and a [super-app was integrated into the rapid-transit network](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/senegal-sonatel-connects-max-it-app-to-dakar-bus-rapid-transit-network) for ticketing. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Both ends of the regional share come from the same source's own series rather than from movement in the base, and eleven electronic-money issuers operate in the country ([share](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2026-03/Rapport%20annuel%20sur%20les%20services%20financiers%20num%C3%A9riques%20dans%20l%27UEMOA%20-%202024.pdf)).<br><br>A super-app was integrated into the rapid-transit network for ticketing and top-ups; the prior digital-payment figure carries no date, and the network runs up to 300,000 passengers a day ([transit](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/senegal-sonatel-connects-max-it-app-to-dakar-bus-rapid-transit-network)).</details> | Advanced |
| Sierra Leone | A [prepaid card requiring no bank account](https://sierraloaded.sl/business/afrimoney-prepaid-visa-card-launched-sierra-leone/) and a [virtual card through an operator app](https://techafricanews.com/2026/03/31/orange-money-launches-mastercard-in-sierra-leone-with-zenith-bank-and-mastercard-partnership/) both launched in 2026, and [financial inclusion](https://thefintechtimes.com/rebuilding-financial-services-through-fintech-in-sierra-leone/) reaches the base only as a named-analyst assessment. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The prepaid card launched in May 2026 with a bank partner and requires no bank account ([prepaid](https://sierraloaded.sl/business/afrimoney-prepaid-visa-card-launched-sierra-leone/)), and a virtual card launched in March 2026 through an operator application ([virtual](https://techafricanews.com/2026/03/31/orange-money-launches-mastercard-in-sierra-leone-with-zenith-bank-and-mastercard-partnership/)).<br><br>A savings product paying annual interest through an application and short codes launched in November 2025 ([savings](https://sierraleoneconcordtimes.com/mikashboks-and-ecobank-partner-to-deepen-financial-inclusion-in-sierra-leone/)), and a multi-currency wallet and card launched in April 2025 ([wallet](https://techafricanews.com/2025/04/22/velmie-and-metro-cable-launch-vult-sierra-leones-first-multi-currency-digital-wallet/)).<br><br>The inclusion rate itself is a named-analyst assessment rather than an official series, so five products can be dated and the population they reach cannot ([inclusion](https://thefintechtimes.com/rebuilding-financial-services-through-fintech-in-sierra-leone/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Somalia | [Mobile money runs about 155m transactions a month worth US$2.7bn](https://itweb.africa/article/hormuud-anchors-somalias-digital-economy/Olx4zMkaZozv56km) against formal banking reaching about 15 per cent of the population. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The dominant service was re-certified against the industry mobile money standard in August 2025 ([certification](https://www.hiiraan.com/news4/2025/Aug/202622/hormuud_s_evc_plus_earns_gsma_re_certification.aspx)).<br><br>Nationally, mobile money runs about 155 million transactions a month worth US$2.7bn on lender figures, against formal banking reaching about 15 per cent of the population ([scale](https://itweb.africa/article/hormuud-anchors-somalias-digital-economy/Olx4zMkaZozv56km)).<br><br>That ratio is the whole payments picture here: a private mobile-money system carrying a monetary mass many times the reach of the banks, which is the system the instant payment rail and the identity credential are both being built to sit alongside.</details> | Advanced |
| South Africa | One bank reported [digitally active customers up 10% to 3.8m against 9.9m total](https://www.absa.africa/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Results-booklet-for-the-period-ended-30-June-2026-single-page.pdf), and a city is [removing cash from six service counters](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/more-council-centres-go-cashless-in-cape-town/lLn14MmQzbnMJ6Aa) with no stated route for the unbanked. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-08-18 — a large bank's interim results [put digitally active personal and private banking customers at 3.8m, up 10%, against 9.9m total customers in that segment](https://www.absa.africa/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Results-booklet-for-the-period-ended-30-June-2026-single-page.pdf), with total technology spend of R8,779m for the half, up 7% and 28% of group expenses. These are one institution's figures, reported group-wide across seventeen countries, so they bound the cost and pace of migration at a single bank and not the sector.<br><br>2026-08-21 — a second bank [reported 581 self-service kiosks handling more than half a million transactions a month across over 60 services, dispensing almost 70% of its instant-issued debit cards, 56% of them to entry-level clients](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/nedbank-tests-next-gen-self-service-branches/GxwQDq1DPg6MlPVo).<br><br>2026-09-01 — uptake seen from the other side: a city is [extending a cashless pilot to three further service offices, so cash is no longer accepted at six counters](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/more-council-centres-go-cashless-in-cape-town/lLn14MmQzbnMJ6Aa). No figure is published for how many residents at those counters paid in cash before the change, and nothing is stated about an alternative for the unbanked — which is the whole of the inclusion question a cashless counter raises. No regulator series covers any of this.</details> | Advanced |
| South Sudan | [Mobile money operators pledged nationwide agent expansion](https://www.onecitizendaily.com/index.php/2025/08/15/mobile-money-providers-pledge-nationwide-expansion-to-support-digital-payment/) with the central bank, and [1,225 entrepreneurs graduated](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/24/mtn-momo-equips-1225-entrepreneurs-digital-finance-skills/) from an operator training programme. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The expansion pledge was made in August 2025 and no agent count, coverage figure or subsequent report is held ([pledge](https://www.onecitizendaily.com/index.php/2025/08/15/mobile-money-providers-pledge-nationwide-expansion-to-support-digital-payment/), [safeguard](https://www.onecitizendaily.com/index.php/2025/08/29/boss-vows-to-safeguard-robust-payment-system/)).<br><br>The training graduation is the operator's own account, with no seed-funding total and no measure of what the training changed ([training](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/24/mtn-momo-equips-1225-entrepreneurs-digital-finance-skills/)).<br><br>Against an economy that remains overwhelmingly cash-based, neither a pledge nor a graduation count establishes uptake.</details> | No change |
| Sudan | One bank's [mobile money application is the dominant retail rail](https://www.aljazeera.net/ebusiness/2026/7/31/banking-apps-currency-sudan), driven by cash scarcity rather than technology uptake, and [short-code banking](https://techafricanews.com/2026/03/18/sudan-launches-ussd-banking-services-to-expand-digital-financial-access/) was launched to reach users without internet. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The dominant application carries retail settlement in the capital's markets because cash is scarce and the currency has collapsed, not because of technology uptake; a war-economy analysis finds its volumes now a monetary mass outside central-bank supervision ([dominance](https://www.aljazeera.net/ebusiness/2026/7/31/banking-apps-currency-sudan), [infrastructure](https://voxdev.org/topic/institutions-political-economy/rebuilding-sudans-digital-infrastructure-amidst-conflict), [cash](https://www.calpnetwork.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/CALP-Network-Report-Group-Cash-Transfers-in-Sudan-July-2026.pdf)).<br><br>Short-code banking was launched jointly by the ministry, the central bank and the telecommunications regulator and works without internet access; operational details for participating banks and operators were to be announced separately and none is held ([short code](https://techafricanews.com/2026/03/18/sudan-launches-ussd-banking-services-to-expand-digital-financial-access/)).<br><br>An operator wallet launched in the wartime seat of government covering transfers, airtime, bills, merchant purchases and agent cash handling as phase one of three, and no later report confirms progression ([wallet](https://techafricanews.com/2025/04/18/zain-launches-bede-digital-wallet-to-boost-financial-inclusion-in-sudan/)).</details> | Regressed |
| Tanzania | Active mobile money subscriptions reached 87.05 million in June 2026 ([report](https://www.thecitizen.co.tz/tanzania/news/national/mobile-money-surge-fuels-cashless-growth-5578364)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>Active mobile money subscriptions reached 87.05 million in June 2026, as the country pressed a cashless agenda ([report](https://www.thecitizen.co.tz/tanzania/news/national/mobile-money-surge-fuels-cashless-growth-5578364)). A bank and the stock exchange launched digital share trading through the bank's own channel ([launch](https://dailynews.co.tz/nbc-dse-launch-digital-share-trading-service-through-nbc-kiganjani/)).<br><br>The series is flattening and the regulator's own report draws that conclusion ([index](https://www.bot.go.tz/Publications/Regular/Annual%20Report/en/2026072316300213.pdf), [usage gap](https://ippmedia.co.tz/the-guardian/business/read/tanzanias-financial-inclusion-expands-but-usage-gap-is-becoming-the-next-challenge-2026-07-28-120905)).<br><br>Active mobile-money accounts and the annual transaction count are on different bases and the stated growth rate does not reconcile against the earlier one ([accounts](https://ippmedia.co.tz/the-guardian/business/read/tanzanias-financial-inclusion-expands-but-usage-gap-is-becoming-the-next-challenge-2026-07-28-120905)).<br><br>Financial access points grew through agent build-out rather than branch build-out, and the council says so itself ([access points](https://www.bot.go.tz/Publications/Regular/Annual%20Report/en/2026072316300213.pdf)). A third national inclusion framework was reported on for 2025, with the base holding no framework position at the window's start, no text and no mid-term review ([framework](https://www.bot.go.tz/Publications/Regular/Annual%20Report/en/2026072316300213.pdf)).</details> | Advanced |
| Togo | [Electronic-money accounts](https://www.togofirst.com/fr/finance/1803-18503-togo-le-nombre-de-comptes-de-monnaie-electronique-a-progresse-de-76-87-en-un-an) were measured by the central bank to end-2024 and published in 2026, and [mobile-money subscribers and transaction value](https://www.togofirst.com/fr/telecoms/0807-19499-mobile-money-moov-africa-togo-lance-de-nouveaux-services-pour-renforcer-sa-position) come from regulator figures for the third quarter of 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The central bank observation is two years old at the point of its publication inside the window ([accounts](https://www.togofirst.com/fr/finance/1803-18503-togo-le-nombre-de-comptes-de-monnaie-electronique-a-progresse-de-76-87-en-un-an)), and the regulator figures reach the base relayed through a report on an operator product launch ([subscribers](https://www.togofirst.com/fr/telecoms/0807-19499-mobile-money-moov-africa-togo-lance-de-nouveaux-services-pour-renforcer-sa-position)).<br><br>A new wallet entered service in October 2025 with a partner bank under regional electronic-money rules, built on the operator's acquisition of a foreign fintech, with no user or transaction figure on file ([wallet](https://www.togofirst.com/fr/finance/1510-17308-mobile-money-au-togo-gozem-money-entre-en-service)), and an operator extended its mobile-money services at a launch event in July 2026 ([extension](https://www.togofirst.com/fr/telecoms/0807-19499-mobile-money-moov-africa-togo-lance-de-nouveaux-services-pour-renforcer-sa-position)).<br><br>Neither uptake measure is current and neither product carries a figure, so the direction of use here is not establishable.</details> | Advanced |
| Tunisia | [Mobile payments rose 81 per cent](https://trendsnafrica.com/central-bank-of-tunisia-reports-81-surge-in-mobile-payments-as-tunisia-accelerates-its-digital-transformation-in-2025/) in 2025 on central bank figures, and the [active wallet count](https://trendsnafrica.com/central-bank-of-tunisia-reports-81-surge-in-mobile-payments-as-tunisia-accelerates-its-digital-transformation-in-2025/) diverges by a factor of about 1.7 between two reporting routes. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The postal operator provided digital wallets free to students for the 2026-2027 university registration ([report](https://www.lapresse.tn/2026/08/31/inscription-universitaire-2026-2027-la-poste-tunisienne-met-gratuitement-a-disposition-des-etudiants-des-portefeuilles-numeriques/)). No take-up figure is stated.<br><br>The mobile payment and licensed-provider figures are the central bank's full-year 2025 numbers, reaching the base through an aggregator's repackaging rather than the bulletin itself ([payments](https://trendsnafrica.com/central-bank-of-tunisia-reports-81-surge-in-mobile-payments-as-tunisia-accelerates-its-digital-transformation-in-2025/), [providers](https://trendsnafrica.com/central-bank-of-tunisia-reports-81-surge-in-mobile-payments-as-tunisia-accelerates-its-digital-transformation-in-2025/)).<br><br>The base carries 815,000 wallets for 2025 from that aggregator ([wallets](https://trendsnafrica.com/central-bank-of-tunisia-reports-81-surge-in-mobile-payments-as-tunisia-accelerates-its-digital-transformation-in-2025/)); reporting attributed directly to the bulletins gives roughly 469,000 at end-2025, and only the aggregator's side is held ([wallets](https://trendsnafrica.com/central-bank-of-tunisia-reports-81-surge-in-mobile-payments-as-tunisia-accelerates-its-digital-transformation-in-2025/)).<br><br>A near-doubling in mobile payments and a wallet count that differs by 1.7 times between routes cannot both be read confidently, and the divergence is stated rather than resolved.</details> | Advanced |
| Uganda | [Electronic money transaction value grew 28.6 per cent](https://ubc.go.ug/2025/10/06/bous-new-payment-system-cuts-delays-and-boosts-e-money-by-28-6) with growth in agents and handsets while both screen-based channels contracted, and [active wallet users](https://www.mtn.co.ug/wp-content/uploads/sites/7/2026/08/MTN-Uganda-Sustainability-Report-2025-Digital-1.pdf) rose on the operator's own report. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The growth sits in agents and handsets; both screen-based channels contracted ([value](https://ubc.go.ug/2025/10/06/bous-new-payment-system-cuts-delays-and-boosts-e-money-by-28-6)).<br><br>The operator's two transaction-value figures are not reconcilable and no growth rate is derived from them ([users](https://www.mtn.co.ug/wp-content/uploads/sites/7/2026/08/MTN-Uganda-Sustainability-Report-2025-Digital-1.pdf), [separation](https://itweb.africa/article/mtn-uganda-to-list-momo-fintech-unit/WnpNgM21gQw7VrGd)).<br><br>Shareholders approved separating the money business into a new financial company in July 2025 ([spin-off](https://itweb.africa/article/mtn-uganda-to-list-momo-fintech-unit/WnpNgM21gQw7VrGd)), and the platform migrated to a cloud-native vendor platform across four markets on the vendor's own performance figures ([migration](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/21/ericsson-mtn-complete-momo-cloud-migration-across-four-african-markets/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Zambia | [Financial inclusion among adults](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/from-rails-to-routine/dgp45vaB8nQ7X9l8) rose on a national consumer survey published by the central bank, with the survey month not stated, and [securities exchange app users](https://itweb.africa/article/digital-push-powers-zambian-bourse/lLn14MmQzolMJ6Aa) rose on the exchange's own registration figure. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The inclusion figure is carried at year precision because the survey month is not stated ([inclusion](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/from-rails-to-routine/dgp45vaB8nQ7X9l8)).<br><br>The exchange figure is for registered users, not active traders or funded accounts ([app users](https://itweb.africa/article/digital-push-powers-zambian-bourse/lLn14MmQzolMJ6Aa)).<br><br>Registered rather than active, and a survey with no stated month, are the two qualifications every uptake claim in this unit carries.</details> | Advanced |
| Zimbabwe | [Electronic retail transaction value and volume](https://bullszimbabwe.com/digital-payments-surge-but-adoption-lags-survey/) rose in the first half of 2026 on figures in different currencies over different periods, and [cards issued far exceed cards in use](https://bullszimbabwe.com/digital-payments-surge-but-adoption-lags-survey/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The two transaction figures are in different currencies over different periods and are not directly comparable ([transactions](https://bullszimbabwe.com/digital-payments-surge-but-adoption-lags-survey/), [growth](https://techafricanews.com/2025/09/15/zimbabwes-digital-finance-sector-sees-explosive-growth-driven-by-government-reforms/)).<br><br>The cards figure is a survey quoted by the researcher with no base, sample or method published ([cards](https://bullszimbabwe.com/digital-payments-surge-but-adoption-lags-survey/)), and national payment system value rose on June 2026 monthly figures, with the May volume derived from a reported fall rather than stated directly ([system value](https://fingaz.co.zw/2026/08/20/digital-payments-surge-17pct/)).<br><br>A national financial inclusion strategy runs 2022 to 2026, citing formally served adults rising from 69 per cent in 2014 to 83 per cent in 2022 against a 90 per cent target ([inclusion](https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/zim220712.pdf)).</details> | Mixed, electronic transaction value surging while cards issued far exceed cards in use |

## Cross-border functionality

| Country | Developments | Progress |
|---|---|---|
| Algeria | The central bank [joined the pan-African settlement network as its eighteenth member on 15 August 2025](https://www.afreximbank.com/bank-of-algeria-joins-papss-network-accelerating-financial-integration-in-africa-as-algeria-prepares-to-host-iatf-2025); no transaction volume is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Membership did not exist at the window's opening. 2025-08-15 - the central bank [joined as the eighteenth country, with no transaction volume held](https://www.afreximbank.com/bank-of-algeria-joins-papss-network-accelerating-financial-integration-in-africa-as-algeria-prepares-to-host-iatf-2025).<br><br>No Algerian bank is named as live on the rail, no corridor volume or value is published, and nothing states the settlement or pricing arrangement a domestic institution would face. It is an accession rather than a working corridor, and it sits in a market whose own domestic instant rail has not launched.</details> | Advanced |
| Angola | The kwanza [became the second settlement currency in the regional payment system on 27 July 2026](https://www.sadc.int/latest-news/angolan-kwanza-introduced-sadc-rtgs-system), against about US$3.77bn of flows with member states. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-07-28 — the two central-bank governors [formally introduced the kwanza into the regional real-time settlement system, the second settlement currency in its history, against 2025 trade and interbank flows with the other fourteen member states of about US$3.77bn across nine currencies](https://www.sadc.int/latest-news/angolan-kwanza-introduced-sadc-rtgs-system), after [integration was announced in May](https://angop.ao/noticias/economia/kwanza-vai-integrar-sistema-de-pagamento-da-sadc-a-partir-do-ii-semestre/). The system had cleared in one currency since 2013.<br><br>No volume settled in kwanza has been published since, and no retail cross-border service appears anywhere on this ledger: this is interbank settlement, not remittances or merchant payments. What it changes is which currency regional trade can be invoiced and cleared in, and the test of it will be a settled-value series that does not yet exist.</details> | Advanced |
| Botswana | An instant regional corridor [went live inbound from two neighbours in March 2026](https://techafricanews.com/2026/03/02/payincs-tcib-unlocks-real-time-cross-border-payments-between-south-africa-and-botswana/), and [card e-commerce acceptance was extended across nine markets](https://itweb.africa/article/standard-bank-expands-unionpay-acceptance/KBpdgvpmBA47LEew). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-03-02 — [an instant cross-border corridor went live inbound from South Africa and Zimbabwe](https://techafricanews.com/2026/03/02/payincs-tcib-unlocks-real-time-cross-border-payments-between-south-africa-and-botswana/), [rolled through a commercial bank](https://www.sundaystandard.info/first-capital-bank-rolls-out-instant-sadc-payments/), where no live regional instant corridor into the country existed. No volume or value figure for the corridor is held.<br><br>2026-08-19 — [online acceptance of a foreign card scheme opened to participating merchants across nine African markets, settling in local currency, dollars or both](https://itweb.africa/article/standard-bank-expands-unionpay-acceptance/KBpdgvpmBA47LEew), [extending a collaboration running since 2024](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/19/unionpay-and-standard-bank-expand-e-commerce-acceptance-across-nine-african-markets/). Nearly 900 participating merchants across the nine markets and about 7 million cards issued in Africa are the two companies' own figures, with no Botswana-specific count.<br><br>Cross-border rails are ahead of domestic ones here, which is the reverse of the usual order and follows from the absent national switch.</details> | Advanced |
| Burundi | The central bank [acceded to the pan-African settlement system with four banks authorised](https://www.jimberemag.org/une-revolution-de-leconomie-digitale-au-burundi/) and [expects to join the East African payment system by December 2026](https://www.theeastafrican.co.ke/tea/news/east-africa/burundi-to-join-east-african-payment-network-by-december-2026-5562616). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-08-06 — [the central bank has acceded to the Pan-African Payment and Settlement System, with four Burundian banks authorised to participate as at 4 August 2026](https://www.jimberemag.org/une-revolution-de-leconomie-digitale-au-burundi/). The accession date itself is not stated, only that it has occurred, and [the continental trade secretariat discussed the system as an implementation instrument during its August mission](https://au-afcfta.org/2026/08/afcfta-secretary-general-begins-official-mission-to-burundi/).<br><br>2026-08-19 — [the bank told the region's monetary affairs committee it expects to be inside the East African Payment System by December 2026, having migrated its settlement infrastructure to the international messaging standard](https://www.theeastafrican.co.ke/tea/news/east-africa/burundi-to-join-east-african-payment-network-by-december-2026-5562616), and [has begun connecting domestic banks, the post office and the central bank to the COMESA regional payment and settlement system through technical training](https://www.channelafrica.co.za/channelafrica/news/burundi-integrates-comesa-payment-systems-to-boost-cross-border-trade/). [Regional acceleration of the payment system was pressed at the July governors' meeting](https://www.rtnb.bi/?mypost?id=529).<br><br>Two accessions and one expectation, with no go-live date, participant list or transaction volume published for any of them.</details> | Advanced |
| Cameroon | The [regional central bank joined the pan-African settlement system in July 2026](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/09/beac-joins-papss-to-strengthen-cross-border-payments-in-central-africa/), extending it to all six member states. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-07-09 - the regional central bank [joined the pan-African settlement system, extending it to all six member states and 72m people and taking the network to 28 countries and sixteen switches](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/09/beac-joins-papss-to-strengthen-cross-border-payments-in-central-africa/), with [member-state banks to be integrated by the end of 2026](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-finance/1007-140077-les-banques-de-la-cemac-seront-integrees-au-systeme-de-paiement-papss-d-ici-fin-2026).<br><br>No Cameroonian bank is yet named as live on the rail, no corridor volume or value is published, and nothing states the pricing or settlement arrangement a Cameroonian institution would face. Membership is an accession, not a working corridor.</details> | Advanced |
| Chad | The regional central bank [acceded to the continental payment and settlement system](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-finance/1007-140077-les-banques-de-la-cemac-seront-integrees-au-systeme-de-paiement-papss-d-ici-fin-2026) with member-state banks to connect by end-2026, and a [regional interoperable switch](https://leconomie.info/article/paiements-numeriques-gimacpay-enregistre-203-millions-de-transactions-pour-863-milliards-de-fcfa-en-2025) published 2025 figures. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Accession was announced in July 2026; no Chadian bank is named and the end-2026 target is untested ([accession](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-finance/1007-140077-les-banques-de-la-cemac-seront-integrees-au-systeme-de-paiement-papss-d-ici-fin-2026)).<br><br>The regional switch presented annual figures for 2025 at a regional meeting, and no country-disaggregated figure for it exists in this base ([switch](https://leconomie.info/article/paiements-numeriques-gimacpay-enregistre-203-millions-de-transactions-pour-863-milliards-de-fcfa-en-2025), [merchant](https://leconomie.info/paiement-marchand-moins-de-1-des-transactions-interoperables-enregistrees-en-zone-cemac-au-premier-trimestre-2026/)).<br><br>A regional interoperable code standard launched under a monetary union regulation of April 2026; no domestic bank, wallet or merchant deployment is on record, so the country is covered by the regulation rather than shown live ([standard](https://leconomie.info/article/paiement-numerique-le-gimac-officialise-la-norme-qr-code-interoperable-de-la-cemac)).</details> | Advanced |
| Comoros | [Implementation of accession to the pan-African settlement system commenced in August 2024](https://www.afreximbank.com/central-bank-of-comoros-commencement-of-activities-for-the-implementation-of-papss-in-comoros) and nothing has followed. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Implementation [commenced on 23 August 2024, the membership agreement having been signed in July 2024, with commercial banks to begin integrating](https://www.afreximbank.com/central-bank-of-comoros-commencement-of-activities-for-the-implementation-of-papss-in-comoros).<br><br>No bank integration, transaction volume or go-live is recorded in the base since. Two years with no follow-up source is itself the finding, in a country whose domestic interbank settlement only entered service in April 2026 - the cross-border rail was joined before the domestic one existed, and neither now has a published corridor figure.</details> | No change |
| Congo | An [instant-transfer application opened a corridor from two European countries](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/belmoney-digipay-launch-instant-transfer-app-for-congo-and-drc), targeting the international cost cap. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-05 - an application [launched on a remittances-as-a-service model, live from two European countries into this country and its neighbour, targeting the international cost cap, with the payment institution licensed by a European central bank](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/belmoney-digipay-launch-instant-transfer-app-for-congo-and-drc).<br><br>No volume, achieved price or user figure is published, so whether the cap is met is unestablished. It is the only cross-border retail instrument on this ledger, and it is licensed abroad rather than here - no domestic authorisation, reporting obligation or supervisory position is on file for it.</details> | Advanced |
| Cote d'Ivoire | A [privately built application moves funds between mobile-money networks across six West African countries](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/tech-stars/avec-simocash-l-ivoirien-wilfried-kouame-interconnecte-les-reseaux-d-argent-mobile) with instant cross-border transfers. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-05 - a [privately built application went live moving funds directly between mobile-money networks, in six West African countries, with instant cross-border transfers](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/tech-stars/avec-simocash-l-ivoirien-wilfried-kouame-interconnecte-les-reseaux-d-argent-mobile). Its volumes, licensing and regulatory status are not stated, which for a cross-border money movement route is the material gap.<br><br>The country is [the largest single share of the monetary union's electronic-money accounts, at 40.07%, and carries 34.7% of the union's transaction value](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2026-03/Rapport%20annuel%20sur%20les%20services%20financiers%20num%C3%A9riques%20dans%20l%27UEMOA%20-%202024.pdf) - the volume any regional route would be built on. Nothing on file reports a corridor volume, a remittance figure or a bilateral arrangement with a neighbouring market.</details> | Advanced |
| Djibouti | A [cross-border payments partnership with an Ethiopian operator was signed in September 2025](https://capitalethiopia.com/2025/09/21/santimpay-meras-djibouti-join-hands-to-strengthen-financial-services/); nothing is in service on record. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-09-21 - an [Ethiopian payments operator and a Djiboutian financial-services provider signed a partnership on cross-border digital payments, remittances and merchant solutions, witnessed by the finance, innovation and defence ministers, the startup commissioner and a World Bank representative](https://capitalethiopia.com/2025/09/21/santimpay-meras-djibouti-join-hands-to-strengthen-financial-services/).<br><br>2026-02-11 - the regional body [issued terms of reference to develop a harmonised regional payment-interoperability framework covering settlement, clearing, mobile money and fintech gateways](https://igad.int/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/ToR-IGAD-Payments-Interoperability-V6-1.pdf), an enabling step rather than an operating arrangement.<br><br>Neither is in service. No corridor volume, no launch date and no regulatory approval for either appears on file.</details> | Advanced |
| DR Congo | A [live use case settles mobile-wallet top-ups in stablecoins](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/06/visa-m-pesa-africa-and-onafriq-launch-stablecoin-pilot-in-dr-congo/), bypassing correspondent banking. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-07 - a card-network executive [confirmed on record a live use case settling wallet top-ups in stablecoins in the background, bypassing correspondent banking, which carries about 8% of sub-Saharan remittance cost](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/06/visa-m-pesa-africa-and-onafriq-launch-stablecoin-pilot-in-dr-congo/). The [dollar peg is inferred rather than disclosed](https://www.theeastafrican.co.ke/tea/business-tech/visa-tests-stablecoins-in-drc-for-cross-border-payments-5515040), and a joint pilot-launch framing was downgraded on reconciliation.<br><br>It runs against the central bank's own de-dollarisation deadline of April 2027, and no supervisory position, licence or reporting obligation for stablecoin settlement is on file. No corridor volume or value is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Egypt | The continental settlement system is [headquartered in Cairo with no Egyptian participation, volume or value figure published](https://www.trade.gov/market-intelligence/pan-african-payment-and-settlement-system). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The system has [operated from Cairo since January 2022, connecting the real-time gross settlement systems of participating African central banks, with twenty-five large commercial banks connected in 2022](https://www.trade.gov/market-intelligence/pan-african-payment-and-settlement-system), and no Egyptian participation, volume or value figure has been published in anything held. The [lender behind it continues to frame it as the route to continental trade](https://www.dawan.africa/news/afreximbank-banks-on-digital-payments).<br><br>The only movement on the record is diplomatic: the central bank offering its accession experience to another state in July 2026. Inbound remittances now credit instantly at all banks through the domestic instant rail, which is the cross-border function actually measurable here, and it runs outside the continental system.</details> | No change |
| Equatorial Guinea | Cross-border capability is regional and prudential: the [deposit insurance ceiling rose to 8m CFA](https://www.droitmediasfinance.com/index.php/actualites/droit-bancaire/1165-cemac-i-cobac-avec-la-recente-reforme-du-fogadac-le-plafond-dindemnisation-des-deposants-passe-de-5-a-8-millions-de-fcfa) from December 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The base holds no Equatoguinean cross-border payment instrument. What moved is the regional safety net: the [deposit insurance ceiling rose to 8m CFA for individuals and 15m for firms from 10 December 2025](https://www.droitmediasfinance.com/index.php/actualites/droit-bancaire/1165-cemac-i-cobac-avec-la-recente-reforme-du-fogadac-le-plafond-dindemnisation-des-deposants-passe-de-5-a-8-millions-de-fcfa).<br><br>The country's share of regional electronic payment volume sits on the ledger as its own measure. No participation in a continental settlement system is recorded.</details> | No change |
| Eswatini | [Migration of regional low-value flows to a bloc settlement system](https://itweb.africa/article/egypt-eswatini-deepen-banking-cooperation/KWEBb7yL4wbvmRjO) is reported under way with no timetable, and [accession to a continental payment system](https://itweb.africa/article/egypt-eswatini-deepen-banking-cooperation/KWEBb7yL4wbvmRjO) was discussed with a bilateral partner. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The migration is reported as under way with no timetable, volume, value or mandating instrument given ([migration](https://itweb.africa/article/egypt-eswatini-deepen-banking-cooperation/KWEBb7yL4wbvmRjO)).<br><br>The central bank governor discussed leveraging another country's experience to accelerate continental accession ([accession](https://itweb.africa/article/egypt-eswatini-deepen-banking-cooperation/KWEBb7yL4wbvmRjO)).<br><br>Both are cross-border commitments stated in the same bilateral meeting account, and neither has a date.</details> | Advanced |
| Ethiopia | International card acceptance [went live through the national switch](https://capitalethiopia.com/2025/11/30/ethswitch-unveils-ambitious-15-year-digital-payment-vision-in-strategic-overhaul/) in November 2025, and the payments strategy [proposes first outbound transfers](https://www.ena.et/web/eng/ethiopia-ndps-2026-2030). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The national switch and an international card network brought international acceptance live in November 2025 ([card acceptance](https://capitalethiopia.com/2025/11/30/ethswitch-unveils-ambitious-15-year-digital-payment-vision-in-strategic-overhaul/)).<br><br>The payments strategy published a fortnight later proposes the country's first outbound cross-border transfers, which are not currently permitted ([payments strategy](https://www.ena.et/web/eng/ethiopia-ndps-2026-2030)).<br><br>No transaction volume, corridor or remittance figure is held, and the base records nothing on inbound remittance channels, which are the larger cross-border flow.</details> | Advanced |
| Gabon | The regional central bank [acceded to the pan-African settlement system](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-finance/1007-140077-les-banques-de-la-cemac-seront-integrees-au-systeme-de-paiement-papss-d-ici-fin-2026) in July 2026, against a target of connecting member-state banks by end-2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Accession was announced with integration work not started on the record ([accession](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-finance/1007-140077-les-banques-de-la-cemac-seront-integrees-au-systeme-de-paiement-papss-d-ici-fin-2026)).<br><br>Within the region the two Gabonese mobile-money operators lead issuing: one first with 1,969,325 transactions worth FCFA 68.1bn in the first half of 2026 and the other second with 1,950,401 transactions worth FCFA 55.8bn ([issuing position](https://leconomie.info/article/monetique-moov-gabon-devient-le-premier-emetteur-de-paiements-electroniques-de-la-cemac)). On acquiring, the first is third behind two Cameroonian operators, with 758,629 transactions ([the same figures](https://leconomie.info/article/monetique-moov-gabon-devient-le-premier-emetteur-de-paiements-electroniques-de-la-cemac)).<br><br>The figures are those the outlet states it consulted, and no publication by the regional switch is held.</details> | Advanced |
| Gambia | [Six commercial banks are live](https://papss.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/PAPSS-LIST-OF-LIVE-COMMERCIAL-BANKS_APRIL2026.pdf) on the regional settlement system as at April 2026, with no mobile-wallet rail listed. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [operator's own list](https://papss.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/PAPSS-LIST-OF-LIVE-COMMERCIAL-BANKS_APRIL2026.pdf) names six Gambian banks live in April 2026. The rail is bank-to-bank: no Gambian mobile wallet appears on it, which is the gap that matters in a market where [registered mobile money accounts exceed the population](https://www.cbg.gm/downloads-file/232cd73c-132c-11f1-8725-02e599c15748).<br><br>Cross-border retail moves separately through roaming rather than payments: [caps between The Gambia and Senegal start on 1 March 2026](https://pura.gm/pura-and-artp-sign-mou-to-implement-ecowas-roaming-regulation/).</details> | Advanced |
| Ghana | A [state-majority bank is the designated national gateway](https://www.citinewsroom.com/2026/07/gcb-bank-papss-deepen-partnership-to-accelerate-africas-cross-border-payments-revolution/) to the pan-African settlement system, and direct renminbi settlement opened in August 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A second bank went live in-app and a naira corridor opened from February 2026, from a fintech-routed connection under a six-month central-bank pilot; nothing is quantified, with no volumes, tariff, corridor list or designation date ([gateway](https://www.citinewsroom.com/2026/07/gcb-bank-papss-deepen-partnership-to-accelerate-africas-cross-border-payments-revolution/), [in-app](https://www.graphic.com.gh/business/business-news/prudential-bank-activates-papss-on-mobile-app-to-drive-intra-african-payments.html), [pilot](https://fintech.global/2025/06/23/onafriq-and-papss-launch-cross-border-payments-in-ghana)).<br><br>Direct settlement in renminbi from cedi accounts, in real time or batch and without correspondent-bank intermediation, was announced on 4 August 2026 following central-bank approval ([renminbi](https://www.myjoyonline.com/stanbic-bank-becomes-first-bank-in-ghana-to-offer-direct-chinese-yuan-settlement-via-cips/)).<br><br>Online card acceptance for one international network extended to nine African markets, settling in local currency, dollars or both, with nearly 900 participating merchants across the nine and no country-specific count given ([card acceptance](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/19/unionpay-and-standard-bank-expand-e-commerce-acceptance-across-nine-african-markets/)).<br><br>The central bank digital currency remains at pilot stage with its focus pivoted to cross-border settlement for sub-regional trade and no rollout date ([pilot](https://www.graphic.com.gh/business/business-news/bog-keeps-e-cedi-alive-o-shifts-focus-to-cross-border-payments.html), [not abandoned](https://www.myjoyonline.com/bog-has-not-abandoned-ecedi-project-bog-governor-assures/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Guinea | The country is a [participant in the pan-African settlement system with no transaction recorded](https://www.bcrg.gn/projets-de-reformes-en-cours-a-travers-la-dsmp/), and the regional payment project was withdrawn. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Participant status carries no recorded transaction on the platform ([project register](https://www.bcrg.gn/projets-de-reformes-en-cours-a-travers-la-dsmp/)).<br><br>The regional integrated payment project was withdrawn from the central bank's 2025 strategic priorities after a single evaluation meeting in July 2024, with no visibility on next steps.<br><br>Both readings come from the same undated register, so the country's cross-border position is established entirely by one internal document.</details> | No change |
| Guinea-Bissau | Cross-border capability is the monetary union's; no national instrument is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Guinea-Bissau's payment rails are union rails, and the base holds no national cross-border instrument. The [union register of authorised establishments](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/inline-files/4%20-%20Liste%20Guin%C3%A9e-Bissau_2026.pdf) is the operative list.<br><br>What is being built across borders is documents rather than money: the [paperless customs directive and trade-document platform presented at Bissau in April 2026](https://ang.gw/uemoa-bissau-acolhe-workshop-de-apresentacao-do-projecto-de-plataforma-eletronica-de-trocas-de-documentos-comerciais-no-espaco-comunitario/).</details> | No change |
| Kenya | A [national switch links more than 80 institutions](https://thepaypers.com/payments/news/) to the pan-African settlement system, the first such national-switch pilot. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A commercial bank group went live on 11 August 2026, adding its branch network to instant cross-border transfers in local currencies ([go-live](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/pan-african-payment-settlement-system_papss-papss-abcbank-activity-7492934194994774016-TGoX)).<br><br>Online card acceptance for one international network extended to nine African markets, settling in local currency, dollars or both, with nearly 900 participating merchants across the nine and no country-specific count given ([card acceptance](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/19/unionpay-and-standard-bank-expand-e-commerce-acceptance-across-nine-african-markets/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Lesotho | [Renminbi settlement](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/27/standard-bank-surpasses-cny-8-billion-in-china-cross-border-payment-transactions/) became available through a commercial bank in July 2026, while [migrant workers moved to fintechs](https://techcabal.com/2026/06/12/south-africa-remittance-rules-migrants/) as South African rules tightened. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Standard Bank extended access to China's cross-border interbank payment system beyond South Africa to five markets including Lesotho, reported on 27 July 2026 ([access](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/27/standard-bank-surpasses-cny-8-billion-in-china-cross-border-payment-transactions/)).<br><br>Migrant workers remitting from South Africa moved from banks to fintechs as South African remittance and banking rules tightened, reported in June 2026 ([remittances](https://techcabal.com/2026/06/12/south-africa-remittance-rules-migrants/)). No corridor volume, price or regional switch connection is published.</details> | Mixed, a new renminbi settlement route against migrant workers pushed out of bank channels |
| Liberia | Liberia has been [live on the regional settlement system](https://www.cbl.org.lr/media/features-articles/eg-saamoi-speaks-joint-launch-fined-papss-monrovia) since March 2025, with the central bank as settlement agent. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [governor's remarks at the rollout of 27 March 2025](https://www.cbl.org.lr/media/features-articles/eg-saamoi-speaks-joint-launch-fined-papss-monrovia) record Liberia live on the Pan-African Payment and Settlement System with the central bank as the national settlement agent.<br><br>The bank's [2025 annual report](https://www.cbl.org.lr/sites/default/files/documents/CENTRAL%20BANK%20OF%20LIBERIA%20ANNUAL%20REPORT%202025.pdf) confirms implementation and the official launch in 2025, and publishes the first series of cross-border transfers terminating into mobile wallets — the base's first evidence of cross-border value arriving in a Liberian wallet rather than a bank account.</details> | Advanced |
| Libya | [Foreign-currency Visa acceptance](https://libyaherald.com/2026/06/central-bank-of-libya-governor-inaugurates-2nd-libya-international-electronic-payment-exhibition-epaix-2026) went live on local terminals in June 2026, and banks are reported set to join [China's CIPS network](https://www.scmp.com/news/china/diplomacy/article/3364171/why-chinese-yuan-could-soon-be-africas-most-important-currency). <details><summary>Full record</summary>Visa acceptance in foreign currency was launched on local points of sale at EPAIX on 17 June 2026, giving cards issued abroad a route into the domestic network ([launch](https://libyaherald.com/2026/06/central-bank-of-libya-governor-inaugurates-2nd-libya-international-electronic-payment-exhibition-epaix-2026)).<br><br>A newspaper account of 16 August 2026 reports Libyan banks set to join China's Cross-Border Interbank Payment System after talks between the two central banks; no accession date, participant list or signed agreement is stated, and no central-bank statement is held ([account](https://www.scmp.com/news/china/diplomacy/article/3364171/why-chinese-yuan-could-soon-be-africas-most-important-currency)).</details> | Advanced |
| Madagascar | A [card and cross-border partnership was announced](https://www.mastercard.com/news/eemea/en/newsroom/press-releases/en/2025-1/november/mastercard-and-axian-digibank-fintech-join-to-advance-greater-financial-inclusion-across-africa/) in November 2025 across five markets, with no launch date for Madagascar. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A card network and the domestic mobile money group announced a collaboration in November 2025 to launch virtual and physical cards and cross-border payment features under the domestic mobile money brands across Tanzania, Madagascar, Togo, Comoros and Senegal ([announcement](https://www.mastercard.com/news/eemea/en/newsroom/press-releases/en/2025-1/november/mastercard-and-axian-digibank-fintech-join-to-advance-greater-financial-inclusion-across-africa/)).<br><br>What the base does not hold is a live cross-border service, a corridor volume, a regional settlement arrangement Madagascar participates in, or a date for the announced features reaching the domestic market.</details> | Advanced |
| Malawi | A [regional retail payments corridor with Zambia](https://mwnation.com/comesa-payment-system-connects-malawi-zambia) is the first interoperable cross-border system in local currencies, with a scheme rulebook published in August 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The corridor links the two countries in local currencies without foreign exchange, supervised by both central banks, with three Malawian early adopters ([corridor](https://mwnation.com/comesa-payment-system-connects-malawi-zambia)).<br><br>The clearing house published the scheme rulebook setting operational and governance rules for instant low-cost cross-border retail payments, alongside a gender audit and strategy to 2030 drawing on research along the corridor ([rulebook](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/comesa-clearing-house_comesaclearinghouse-drpp-regionalintegration-activity-7492550470780182528-vX_W)). No volume or value figure for the corridor is held.</details> | Advanced |
| Mali | A [remittance integration into mobile wallets](https://www.finextra.com/pressarticle/105780/terrapay-enables-cross-border-remittances-for-wave-mobile-money-users-in-mali) was announced in May 2025 and no volume has been published since. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The integration between a cross-border payments network and a wallet operator was announced in May 2025, vendor-reported ([integration](https://www.finextra.com/pressarticle/105780/terrapay-enables-cross-border-remittances-for-wave-mobile-money-users-in-mali)).<br><br>No corridor, volume, price or count of participating institutions is published, and no regional switch connection for the country is separately recorded.</details> | No change |
| Mauritius | An [India payment link](https://lexpress.mu/node/554033) has run since 2024, a [Seychelles corridor](https://platformafrica.com/2026/07/16/from-connectivity-to-intelligence-how-mauritius-telecom-is-building-the-intelligent-layer-of-everyday-mauritius/) was announced in July 2026, and the rupee is [reported as considered](https://www.centralbanking.com/central-banks/currency/7976492/southern-african-countries-consider-joining-sadc-rtgs) for regional settlement. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The bilateral cross-border payments link with India was the country's first, in force since February 2024, with no volume or corridor figure published ([link](https://lexpress.mu/node/554033)).<br><br>A corridor with Seychelles was announced as launched by the state operator at its summit, with no corroborating record from the partner, the central bank or the counterpart central bank on file ([corridor](https://platformafrica.com/2026/07/16/from-connectivity-to-intelligence-how-mauritius-telecom-is-building-the-intelligent-layer-of-everyday-mauritius/)).<br><br>Onboarding the rupee to the regional settlement system is reported as under consideration, potentially by the end of 2027, on a spokesperson's account from another central bank; no domestic statement is held, and the named next currencies are two others ([settlement](https://www.centralbanking.com/central-banks/currency/7976492/southern-african-countries-consider-joining-sadc-rtgs)).</details> | Advanced |
| Morocco | The central bank [acceded to the continental settlement system on 7 July 2025](https://papss.com/fr/media/bank-al-maghrib-adhere-au-papss-aisant-du-maroc-le-17eme-pays-membre-a-rejoindre-son-reseau/) as its seventeenth member country. <details><summary>Full record</summary>[Accession](https://papss.com/fr/media/bank-al-maghrib-adhere-au-papss-aisant-du-maroc-le-17eme-pays-membre-a-rejoindre-son-reseau/) puts Morocco on the continental rail, and a [global payment network launched real-time payouts in local currency](https://www.thunes.com/news/thunes-launches-real-time-payments-into-morocco/) two months later against a remittance market of US$11.5bn in 2023.<br><br>Exchange control remains the binding rule, and it eased: the [2026 general instruction raised the electronic commerce allowance for accredited start-ups to MAD 2m and the individual ceiling to MAD 20,000](https://www.oc.gov.ma/sites/default/files/2025-12/Release%20IGOC%202026.pdf).</details> | Advanced |
| Mozambique | Six domestic banks [settled 215 transactions worth ZAR 261.79m through the regional real-time system in June 2026](https://www.resbank.co.za/content/dam/sarb/what-we-do/payments-and-settlements/settlement-services/sadc-rtgs/2026-stats/SADC-RTGS%20Settlement%20Statistics%20and%20Indicators%20-%20June%202026.pdf), and the [Angolan kwanza became its second settlement currency in July](https://www.resbank.co.za/en/home/publications/publication-detail-pages/media-releases/2026/kwanza-rtgs). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-07-27 — the Angolan kwanza [was added as the second settlement currency in the regional real-time gross settlement system, after the rand, which had been the only one since 2013](https://www.resbank.co.za/en/home/publications/publication-detail-pages/media-releases/2026/kwanza-rtgs), with further currencies to follow. The country participates as one of fifteen members.<br><br>The connection is demonstrably live rather than merely established: published settlement statistics [record six Mozambican participating banks settling 215 transactions worth ZAR 261.79m in June 2026](https://www.resbank.co.za/content/dam/sarb/what-we-do/payments-and-settlements/settlement-services/sadc-rtgs/2026-stats/SADC-RTGS%20Settlement%20Statistics%20and%20Indicators%20-%20June%202026.pdf).<br><br>2026-08-11 — the central bank [published a notice revising the limits on bank-card payments made abroad, replacing the December 2025 notice](https://www.bancomoc.mz/en/media/highlights/notice-no-4-gbm-2026-establishes-limits-on-payments-abroad-made-using-bank-cards-and-revokes-notice-no-9-gbm-2025-of-9-december/). Regional capacity widened and outbound card capacity was recapped in the same quarter, which is the tension worth reading here.</details> | Advanced, a second settlement currency was added to the regional system and outbound card limits were revised |
| Namibia | The national clearing house [signed to join the regional instant cross-border scheme in March 2026](https://technews.com.na/2026/03/31/namibia-plugged-into-sadc-digital-payments-system-through-namclear-payinc-deal/) and [card e-commerce acceptance](https://itweb.africa/article/standard-bank-expands-unionpay-acceptance/KBpdgvpmBA47LEew) opened in August. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The clearing house agreed with the regional scheme operator to connect local banks and authorised non-banks to the southern African immediate-clearing network, acting as technical gateway for single credit push payments to Common Monetary Area countries; settlement remains deferred, and fraud management, sanctions screening, request-to-pay and bulk real-time payments are named as later phases with no dates ([scheme](https://technews.com.na/2026/03/31/namibia-plugged-into-sadc-digital-payments-system-through-namclear-payinc-deal/)).<br><br>Online acceptance of an international card scheme opened to participating merchants, settling in local currency, dollars or both, as part of an extension to nine African markets; no country-specific merchant count is given ([acceptance](https://itweb.africa/article/standard-bank-expands-unionpay-acceptance/KBpdgvpmBA47LEew)).<br><br>No record of participation in the continental payment and settlement system exists at August 2026, two years after a regional bank targeted central bank sign-up ([system](https://www.namibian.com.na/namibia-can-benefit-from-africa-trade-payment-system)).</details> | Advanced, on two channels while participation in the continental settlement system stays unrecorded |
| Niger | A [mobile-money international transfer service](https://actuniger.com/societe/22299-transfert-dargent-airtel-money-niger-lance-son-service-international-pour-des-operations-simples-et-securisees-au-dela-des-frontieres.html) opened in August 2026 on eight inbound corridors and seven outbound West African destinations. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The mobile-money issuer opened an international transfer service on 25 August 2026, taking inbound transfers from the United States, Canada, Belgium, Germany, China, Turkey, Gabon and Cameroon directly into the recipient's wallet through five remittance partners, and sending outbound transfers to seven West African states ([service](https://actuniger.com/societe/22299-transfert-dargent-airtel-money-niger-lance-son-service-international-pour-des-operations-simples-et-securisees-au-dela-des-frontieres.html)). No volume or fee schedule is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Nigeria | [Continental rail flows fell 53 per cent](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2026/01/nigerias-papss-cross-border-payments-falls-53-to-n5-6bn/) to about US$3.7m in a half-year, while [stablecoin settlement rails](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/20/busha-business-and-tether-partner-to-expand-stablecoin-infrastructure-for-african-businesses/) carry 1,500 businesses and a [regulated naira stablecoin](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/12/nigerias-cngn-stablecoin-goes-live-on-celo-blockchain/) went live on a public blockchain. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A commercial bank put the continental payment system on its app and online channels with no separate onboarding, capped at the local-currency equivalent of US$2,000 a month for individuals ([report](https://starconnectmedia.com/firstbank-papss-and-the-race-to-own-africas-payment-rails/)).<br><br>Flows on the continental rail fell 53 per cent to ₦5.6bn in the first half of 2025 with transactions down 29 per cent to 3,246 ([rail](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2026/01/nigerias-papss-cross-border-payments-falls-53-to-n5-6bn/)); at about US$3.7m for a half-year that reads as a narrow line item rather than national throughput, and the country remains the rail's largest participant ([rail](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2026/01/nigerias-papss-cross-border-payments-falls-53-to-n5-6bn/)). A wallet-based corridor to Ghana entered pilot ([corridor](https://techafricanews.com/2026/02/04/onafriq-and-papss-launch-africas-first-instant-naira-wallet-payments-from-nigeria-to-ghana/)).<br><br>A licensed local exchange plugged into a dollar stablecoin's liquidity for cross-border settlement, reporting more than 1,500 businesses across two countries ([rails](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/20/busha-business-and-tether-partner-to-expand-stablecoin-infrastructure-for-african-businesses/)); the yield partners and rates are undisclosed ([rails](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/20/busha-business-and-tether-partner-to-expand-stablecoin-infrastructure-for-african-businesses/), [settlement](https://itweb.africa/article/stablecoins-test-africas-monetary-future/KBpdg7pmGKnMLEew)).<br><br>The regulated naira stablecoin went live on a public blockchain in August 2026 backed one-to-one, with no supply, holder count or reserve attestation published ([token](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/12/nigerias-cngn-stablecoin-goes-live-on-celo-blockchain/), [listing](https://techafricanews.com/2025/02/10/busha-lists-cngn-expanding-nigerias-digital-economy)); remote diaspora bank onboarding launched in 2025 as remittances rose from US$3.3bn to US$4.73bn ([diaspora](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2025/05/cbn-launches-diaspora-bvn-platform/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Rwanda | A [cross-border payment corridor pilot](https://www.burunditimes.com/new-rwanda-tanzania-payment-pilot-could-transform-trade-across-east-africa/) with a neighbour advanced a proof of concept at a technical meeting, with no launch date set. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A joint cybersecurity and interoperability roadmap for the cross-border instant payment proof of concept with Tanzania was advanced at a technical mission in Kigali ([mission](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/6973/rwanda-and-tanzania-advance-cybersecurity-framework-for-regional-instant-payments)). It remains a proof of concept.<br><br>The proof of concept was advanced at a technical meeting in July 2026 and no launch date is stated ([pilot](https://www.burunditimes.com/new-rwanda-tanzania-payment-pilot-could-transform-trade-across-east-africa/)).<br><br>It sits alongside two licence-passporting memoranda with other neighbours whose texts are unpublished. The country's cross-border payments record is three bilateral arrangements, none of them yet carrying a transaction the base can date.</details> | Advanced |
| Seychelles | The central bank [signed a memorandum with an Indian payments body](https://africabusinessinsight.com/seychelles-upi-launch-2026/) to bring its unified payments interface, with rollout targeted for end-2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The memorandum was signed during a bilateral visit in June 2026 and the source is secondary, with neither party's primary held ([memorandum](https://africabusinessinsight.com/seychelles-upi-launch-2026/)).<br><br>Adopting another country's national payments interface is a substantial architectural commitment — it decides the rails, the standards and the counterparty for cross-border retail payments — and the base holds no text stating what was agreed.</details> | Advanced |
| Somalia | The central bank set [connection to the continental payment and settlement system](https://sonna.so/en/article/Central-Bank-sets-out-plan-to-bring-banks-and-mobile-money-onto-one-payments-network) as a target for before the end of 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The target was stated in July 2026 ([target](https://sonna.so/en/article/Central-Bank-sets-out-plan-to-bring-banks-and-mobile-money-onto-one-payments-network)).<br><br>No connection date, participating institution or corridor is named. It would sit above an instant payment system whose own bank integration sequence was set out in the same statement, so the domestic rail has to complete before the cross-border one can carry anything.</details> | Advanced |
| South Africa | A domestic bank [went live on China's cross-border payment system in November 2025](https://corporateandinvestment.standardbank.com/cib/global/who-we-are/about-us/news/china-cross-border-interbank-payment-system-goes-live-with-standard-bank-as-first-african-bank-to-offer-service) and was [jointly authorised as renminbi clearing bank for Africa in June 2026](https://corporateandinvestment.standardbank.com/cib/global/who-we-are/about-us/news/standard-bank-becomes-the-first-bank-on-the-continent-authorised-to-clear-renminbi-transactions). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-11-20 — a domestic bank [went live and was inaugurated at the central bank as the first African direct participant in China's cross-border interbank payment system](https://corporateandinvestment.standardbank.com/cib/global/who-we-are/about-us/news/china-cross-border-interbank-payment-system-goes-live-with-standard-bank-as-first-african-bank-to-offer-service), with the central bank's governor, the People's Bank governor and the system's chairman present. It was [accredited in April 2025 and licensed in June](https://itweb.africa/article/standard-bank-connects-africa-to-chinas-payment-system/lwrKxq3YVeX7mg1o).<br><br>2026-06-26 — the same bank and a Chinese counterpart were [jointly authorised as renminbi clearing bank for Africa](https://corporateandinvestment.standardbank.com/cib/global/who-we-are/about-us/news/standard-bank-becomes-the-first-bank-on-the-continent-authorised-to-clear-renminbi-transactions), with operational capacity in the nineteen African countries where the group operates. It is the first clearing bank named after a continent and the first jointly operated by two commercial banks.<br><br>Two qualifications the record carries. The nineteen countries are not enumerated in either account. And the designation was made by one foreign central bank, with no African regulator party to it — a continental clearing arrangement constituted entirely outside the continent's own institutions.</details> | Advanced |
