| South Sudan | [Bilateral support for financial technology](https://www.itweb.africa/article/uk-supports-south-sudan-fintech/dgp45qaBlN9vX9l8) was reported in August 2026, with no instrument, envelope, workstream or timetable stated. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The support was reported in August 2026 ([support](https://www.itweb.africa/article/uk-supports-south-sudan-fintech/dgp45qaBlN9vX9l8)).<br><br>The base holds no domestic payment system for it to attach to: the instant rail is at roadmap stage and the payment system bill is at validation workshop.</details> | Advanced |
| Sudan | Two enabling steps — a [card-processing agreement](https://www.network.ae/en/about-us/press-and-media/news/network-international-partners-with-saudi-sudanese-bank-to-accelerate-digital-transformation-in-sudan-s-banking-sector) and a [SWIFT service bureau accreditation](https://ae.linkedin.com/posts/electronic-banking-services-ltd-ebs-_ebs-swift-expandyourchoices-activity-7470784025327030272-ObTh) — against an assessment finding [no connectivity outside Sudan](https://migrantmoney.uncdf.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/05/Sudan-Infrastructure-Assessment-Report-ENG.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The standing assessment of April 2023 found the domestic payment infrastructure had no connectivity outside Sudan, that Sudan did not participate in the regional settlement system despite membership of the trading bloc, and that 92.5 per cent of remitting migrants used informal channels ([assessment](https://migrantmoney.uncdf.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/05/Sudan-Infrastructure-Assessment-Report-ENG.pdf)).<br><br>On 19 January 2026 a bank signed a processing agreement covering end-to-end digital payments, card-scheme sponsorship and prepaid issuing ([agreement](https://www.network.ae/en/about-us/press-and-media/news/network-international-partners-with-saudi-sudanese-bank-to-accelerate-digital-transformation-in-sudan-s-banking-sector)).<br><br>On 11 June 2026 the central bank's payments arm was accredited as a SWIFT service bureau ([accreditation](https://ae.linkedin.com/posts/electronic-banking-services-ltd-ebs-_ebs-swift-expandyourchoices-activity-7470784025327030272-ObTh)).<br><br>Neither is a live corridor. Both are readiness.</details> | Advanced |
| Tanzania | The cross-border instant payment proof of concept with Rwanda gained a joint cybersecurity and interoperability roadmap ([mission](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/6973/rwanda-and-tanzania-advance-cybersecurity-framework-for-regional-instant-payments)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>A joint cybersecurity and interoperability roadmap for the cross-border instant payment proof of concept with Rwanda was advanced at a technical mission in Kigali ([mission](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/6973/rwanda-and-tanzania-advance-cybersecurity-framework-for-regional-instant-payments)). It remains a proof of concept; nothing on the base records a live corridor.<br><br>The corridor's technical meeting ran over five days in July 2026 ([corridor](https://www.thecitizen.co.tz/tanzania/business/eac-advances-tanzania-rwanda-cross-border-digital-payments-integration-5523374)).<br><br>The card acceptance extension covers nine markets, with nearly 900 participating merchants and about 7 million cards issued in Africa given as the two companies' own figures and no country-specific count ([acceptance](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/19/unionpay-and-standard-bank-expand-e-commerce-acceptance-across-nine-african-markets/), [expansion](https://itweb.africa/article/standard-bank-expands-unionpay-acceptance/KBpdgvpmBA47LEew)).</details> | Advanced |
| Togo | An [instant international payment service](https://www.togofirst.com/en/finance/3110-17454-ecobank-launches-instant-international-payment-service-in-togo) launched with the country as its first market, a [full-service payment institution licence](https://techcabal.com/2026/02/17/francophone-weekly-by-techcabal-012/) was granted to a domestic fintech, and a [cross-border payments platform](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/tech-stars/le-togolais-esso-dong-djafalo-interconnecte-banques-mobile-money-et-cryptos) was profiled. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The bank-led service launched in October 2025 with the country as first market, distinct from the central bank's platform ([service](https://www.togofirst.com/en/finance/3110-17454-ecobank-launches-instant-international-payment-service-in-togo)).<br><br>The payment institution licence was granted under the regional instruction in February 2026; the source is captured as a paywalled newsletter body ([licence](https://techcabal.com/2026/02/17/francophone-weekly-by-techcabal-012/)).<br><br>A cross-border payments platform founded in 2024 was profiled by a technology outlet, and no transaction volume, corridor count, licence or customer figure is stated ([platform](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/tech-stars/le-togolais-esso-dong-djafalo-interconnecte-banques-mobile-money-et-cryptos)).</details> | Advanced |
| Uganda | [One commercial bank went live on the continental payment rail](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/pan-african-payment-settlement-system_papss-africasglobalbank-itsallaboutu-activity-7490084196724621313-kD1a), [direct yuan settlement opened](https://ntv.co.ug/news/uganda-china-tradenew-banking-system-opens-direct-yuan-payments) through one bank, and a [stablecoin settlement corridor](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/16/scrypt-expands-stablecoin-settlement-network-across-four-east-african-markets/) opened. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A single commercial bank's subsidiary announced going live on the continental rail in August 2026 ([rail](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/pan-african-payment-settlement-system_papss-africasglobalbank-itsallaboutu-activity-7490084196724621313-kD1a)).<br><br>Direct yuan settlement opened for domestic businesses through one bank's service rather than a national rail, with no volume, fee or central-bank position stated ([yuan](https://ntv.co.ug/news/uganda-china-tradenew-banking-system-opens-direct-yuan-payments)).<br><br>A stablecoin settlement corridor opened in July 2026 ([stablecoin](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/16/scrypt-expands-stablecoin-settlement-network-across-four-east-african-markets/)), and card acceptance was extended to nine African markets with merchant and card counts given as the two companies' own and no country-specific figure ([cards](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/19/unionpay-and-standard-bank-expand-e-commerce-acceptance-across-nine-african-markets/)). Four cross-border arrangements, none of them a national rail.</details> | Advanced |
| Zambia | A [regional retail payments platform](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/comesa-clearing-house_comesaclearinghouse-drpp-regionalintegration-activity-7492550470780182528-vX_W) published its scheme rulebook and a gender strategy, and [currency onboarding to the regional settlement system](https://www.centralbanking.com/central-banks/currency/7976492/southern-african-countries-consider-joining-sadc-rtgs) is attributed to the system's operator rather than the national central bank. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The corridor with a neighbour is the one the platform runs on; no national transaction volume, participant count or fee schedule is published ([platform](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/comesa-clearing-house_comesaclearinghouse-drpp-regionalintegration-activity-7492550470780182528-vX_W)).<br><br>The currency onboarding is a statement of intent attributed to the system's operator, not to the national central bank ([onboarding](https://www.centralbanking.com/central-banks/currency/7976492/southern-african-countries-consider-joining-sadc-rtgs), [switch](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/from-rails-to-routine/dgp45vaB8nQ7X9l8)).<br><br>Card acceptance was extended to nine African markets with merchant and card counts given as the two companies' own and no country-specific figure ([cards](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/19/unionpay-and-standard-bank-expand-e-commerce-acceptance-across-nine-african-markets/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Zimbabwe | [Participation in the continental payment and settlement system](https://bullszimbabwe.com/digital-payments-surge-but-adoption-lags-survey/) is company-reported to a payments conference, and [regional settlement onboarding](https://www.centralbanking.com/central-banks/currency/7976492/southern-african-countries-consider-joining-sadc-rtgs) is attributed to the operating central bank rather than to this one. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The continental participation reaches the base through the switch operator's report to a conference ([participation](https://bullszimbabwe.com/digital-payments-surge-but-adoption-lags-survey/)).<br><br>The regional onboarding intent, cited as potentially by end-2027, is attributed to the operating central bank and nothing establishes a domestic decision ([onboarding](https://www.centralbanking.com/central-banks/currency/7976492/southern-african-countries-consider-joining-sadc-rtgs)).<br><br>Card acceptance was extended to nine African markets with merchant and card counts given as the two companies' own and no country-specific figure ([cards](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/19/unionpay-and-standard-bank-expand-e-commerce-acceptance-across-nine-african-markets/)).</details> | Advanced |

## Consumer protection

| Country | Developments | Progress |
|---|---|---|
| Algeria | The travel foreign-exchange allowance [moved onto a nominative card](https://www.bank-of-algeria.dz/stoodroa/2026/07/Instruction-N%C2%B007-2026-droit-de-change-du-13-07-2026.pdf); no payments consumer-protection instrument is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-07-13 - a central bank instruction [moves the travel foreign-exchange allowance onto a nominative card](https://www.bank-of-algeria.dz/stoodroa/2026/07/Instruction-N%C2%B007-2026-droit-de-change-du-13-07-2026.pdf), quoted verbatim in the reporting; the instrument's own text is not held.<br><br>That is a change to how a payment instrument is issued rather than a protection for the person using it. No complaint channel, chargeback rule, fraud-reimbursement obligation or dispute-resolution route for electronic payments appears anywhere on this ledger, and the payment service provider regime made in 2025 is not stated to carry one.</details> | No change, a first stated position with no earlier account behind it |
| Benin | A financial-sector consumer redress body [was stood up in July 2025](https://fraternite.bj/guichets-automatiques-bancaires-et-mobile-money-enquete-sur-le-calvaire-des-usagers-face-aux-transactions-echouees/) against unresolved complaints about failed mobile-money transactions. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-07-29 — [a financial-sector consumer redress body was established, against complaints about failed mobile-money transactions going unresolved](https://fraternite.bj/guichets-automatiques-bancaires-et-mobile-money-enquete-sur-le-calvaire-des-usagers-face-aux-transactions-echouees/). None existed before it.<br><br>No caseload, resolution rate or decision from it has been published in the thirteen months since, so the body exists and its effect does not appear on the record. That matters against a market of 11.65m active accounts in which one operator holds most of the revenue, reported under uptake: the redress route is the only counterweight on this ledger to a concentration the regulator has not otherwise acted on.</details> | Advanced |
| Botswana | A [mandatory 60-minute crediting rule for electronic funds transfers took effect on 1 February 2026](https://www.bankofbotswana.bw/sites/default/files/press-release-files/Press%20Release%20-%20Enhancement%20to%20the%20clearing%20and%20settlement%20framework%20for%20electronic%20funds%20transfers.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2019 — [the central bank’s payments oversight policy sets consumer-recourse and fair-disclosure objectives](https://www.bankofbotswana.bw/sites/default/files/NPS_Oversight_Policy.pdf).<br><br>2025-12-31 — [an enhancement to the clearing and settlement framework introduced a mandatory 60-minute customer crediting rule and an added settlement window, in effect from 1 February 2026](https://www.bankofbotswana.bw/sites/default/files/press-release-files/Press%20Release%20-%20Enhancement%20to%20the%20clearing%20and%20settlement%20framework%20for%20electronic%20funds%20transfers.pdf).<br><br>No complaints figure, redress statistic or dispute-resolution route for payment users is held.</details> | Advanced |
| Burkina Faso | A financial inclusion agency [created in 2025 is explicitly not a regulator](https://lefaso.net/spip.php?article148215), and consumer education [runs through a partnership with an operator across four cities](https://www.aib.media/guiriko-lanpfi-et-wave-burkina-lancent-une-campagne-de-formation-au-profit-des-consommateurs-des-services-financiers/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-07-28 — [an agency created in 2025 by merging three bodies is operating and explicitly not a regulator, its director general arguing that account counts conceal absent savings, credit and insurance](https://lefaso.net/spip.php?article148215). The base holds no creation decree and no budget line for it, and [it also carries the project-results reporting for the closed inclusion programme](https://www.sidwaya.info/transformation-numerique-inclusion-financiere-et-financement-des-pme-le-bilan-dun-projet-structurant-au-burkina-faso/).<br><br>The education work is delivered with the market it educates about: [a partnership with a payments operator running since 2025 and extended in 2026 to four cities, with 72-hour sessions on budgeting, savings, credit and consumer protection and participants acting as community relays](https://www.aib.media/guiriko-lanpfi-et-wave-burkina-lancent-une-campagne-de-formation-au-profit-des-consommateurs-des-services-financiers/).<br><br>Consumer protection for a payments market delivered by a promotion agency and an operator, rather than by the financial regulator, is the arrangement the record describes, and no complaint, redress or sanction figure exists against it.</details> | Advanced |
| Burundi | The regulator [ordered an operator to make value-added-service subscription genuinely voluntary](https://www.iwacu-burundi.org/interview-avec-pierre-nduwayo-aucun-service-payant-ne-devrait-etre-active-sans-le-consentement-explicite-de-labonne/), and the consumer association reports no significant improvement two months on. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-07-24 — [injunctions were issued requiring genuinely voluntary subscription to value-added services and prior notification of subscribers](https://www.iwacu-burundi.org/interview-avec-pierre-nduwayo-aucun-service-payant-ne-devrait-etre-active-sans-le-consentement-explicite-de-labonne/), where no such instrument existed. The consumer association reports no significant improvement two months on, no sanction is on file, and the operator maintains that nothing activates without a customer's deliberate action.<br><br>It is the only enforcement action of any kind on this ledger, and it came from the telecommunications regulator rather than from the data protection authority the January 2026 law creates, which is not on record as operating. Nothing states how many subscribers were charged, or whether any money was refunded.</details> | Advanced, an injunction with no sanction and no reported improvement |
| Cameroon | The [financial-literacy project closed on 15 July 2026 leaving its comparison platform with no funded data-update mechanism](https://business221.com/2026/07/24/projet-finance-4-all-au-senegal-265-personnes-issues-de-la-societe-civile-formees-a-leducation-financiere-numerique/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-07-15 - the digital financial-literacy project running since June 2024 [closed, leaving a digital financial-services comparison platform with no funded data-update mechanism, offered to both participating states and neither reported as having accepted](https://business221.com/2026/07/24/projet-finance-4-all-au-senegal-265-personnes-issues-de-la-societe-civile-formees-a-leducation-financiere-numerique/). No Cameroon-side trainee count, budget share or partner list is published.<br><br>The separate [bank-fee comparison platform launched in June 2025 covers commercial banks only, and neither committed extension has been recorded as delivered](https://itweb.africa/article/cameroon-boosts-financial-literacy-with-new-digital-platform/Pero3qZ32EZvQb6m): a savings simulator promised by end-2025 and extension to microfinance and insurance promised for 2026 are both unverified.</details> | Regressed |
| Central African Republic | A [community fact-checking network debunked a mobile-money phishing scam](https://kolokoto.com/2026/07/06/alerte-infox-la-direction-dorange-centrafrique-na-pas-organise-un-jeu-concours/); no regulator or operator response is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-07-06 - a community fact-checking network [debunked a messaging-app phishing scam impersonating a mobile-money service with a fake prize contest harvesting phone numbers and personal identification codes](https://kolokoto.com/2026/07/06/alerte-infox-la-direction-dorange-centrafrique-na-pas-organise-un-jeu-concours/).<br><br>The response the base holds is a fact-check by a non-state body. No regulator statement, operator advisory, complaint count, reimbursement rule or remedy for a defrauded user appears anywhere on this ledger, and no consumer-protection instrument covering electronic payments is held.</details> | No change, a first stated position with no earlier account behind it |
| Comoros | A [2017 regulation governs electronic money](https://banque-comores.km/uploads/Loi%20et%20textes%20reglementaire/REGLEMENTS/2017/R%C3%A8glement%20n%C2%B001_2017_BCC_DSBR%20sur%20la%20monnaie%20%C3%A9lectronique.pdf); a [meter-crediting fault cost a presumed 200 million francs](https://www.comoresinfos.net/200-millions-de-francs-de-pertes-presumees-que-sest-il-passe-entre-mvola-et-la-sonelec-a-moheli/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The standing rule is the central bank's [regulation setting the conditions and modalities for carrying on electronic-money activity](https://banque-comores.km/uploads/Loi%20et%20textes%20reglementaire/REGLEMENTS/2017/R%C3%A8glement%20n%C2%B001_2017_BCC_DSBR%20sur%20la%20monnaie%20%C3%A9lectronique.pdf). It is the only consumer-facing instrument in the base, and no complaint route, redress mechanism or supervisory decision under it is published.<br><br>2026-02 - a [licence for a decentralised digital financial institution was granted, placing a planned mobile nano- and micro-lending entity under prudential supervision](https://mondovisione.com/media-and-resources/news/axian-secures-a-license-for-a-digital-first-regulated-financial-institution-in-t-2026224/).<br><br>2026-08 - a [fault between the mobile-money service and the utility's prepaid electricity meters on Moheli let subscribers credit meters without the amounts being collected, with presumed losses of 200 million francs](https://www.comoresinfos.net/200-millions-de-francs-de-pertes-presumees-que-sest-il-passe-entre-mvola-et-la-sonelec-a-moheli/). No remediation or regulatory response is on file.</details> | Mixed, a second regulated digital institution was licensed while a live fault went unremedied on the record |
| Cote d'Ivoire | Retail agents have [charged an unauthorised flat FCFA 100 on every counter transaction since November 2025](https://www.koaci.com/article/2026/07/24/cote-divoire/societe/cote-divoire-taxe-de-100-fcfa-appliquee-sur-les-operations-mobile-money-dans-les-points-de-vente-les-operateurs-complices_198876.html), with no regulator position on file. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Since November 2025 retail agents have [charged a flat FCFA 100 on every deposit or withdrawal regardless of amount, on top of roughly 1% operator pricing on withdrawals](https://www.koaci.com/article/2026/07/24/cote-divoire/societe/cote-divoire-taxe-de-100-fcfa-appliquee-sur-les-operations-mobile-money-dans-les-points-de-vente-les-operateurs-complices_198876.html). No operator tariff or regulator decision authorises it and the regulator's position is not on file.<br><br>2026-07-24 - the agents' union [put four questions to the data-protection authority on whether kiosk agents may demand, photograph or retain customer identity documents, with no response on file as at 5 August 2026](https://ivoirexpress.ci/2026/07/24/donnees-a-caractere-personnel-les-points-de-vente-mobile-money-sont-ils-habilites-conserver-copie-des-pieces-didentite-de-leurs-clients/).<br><br>Both run at the counter, where uptake is highest and where nothing published states what a customer is entitled to refuse or how a complaint is made.</details> | Regressed |
| Djibouti | [No payment-specific consumer regime is held](https://www.journalofficiel.dj/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/code-numerique.pdf): the Code covers data and commerce, the payments law licensing. <details><summary>Full record</summary>This is a searched absence rather than an unexamined one. The [Digital Code, published on 18 September 2025, carries titles on personal-data protection, electronic communications, cybersecurity and digital services](https://www.journalofficiel.dj/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/code-numerique.pdf); none of them is a payments consumer regime.<br><br>The [payment-system law confines itself to licensing, regulation and supervision of providers and to defining electronic money](https://www.journalofficiel.dj/texte-juridique/loi-n118-an-15-7eme-l-portant-creation-dun-systeme-de-paiement-national-sa-reglementation-et-sa-surveillance/).<br><br>No rule on disclosure, fees, redress or dispute resolution for electronic money or digital payments is on file, and no complaint or redress figure is published by the central bank or any provider.</details> | No change |
| Egypt | [Instant-transfer fees stand where they were set in April 2025, at 0.1% of value with a EGP 20 cap](https://gate.ahram.org.eg/News/5145456.aspx). <details><summary>Full record</summary>Pricing has been [0.1% with a minimum of EGP 0.50 and a maximum of EGP 20 since 1 April 2025, plus EGP 0.50 for balance enquiry beyond ten free monthly requests](https://gate.ahram.org.eg/News/5145456.aspx), and no central-bank decision altering the schedule is held.<br><br>The prior free-use position [rested on a board exemption effective 1 January 2025 for three renewable months rather than on an instrument, and lapsed rather than being repealed](https://www.cbe.org.eg/ar/news-publications/news/2024/12/30/12/31/cbe-extending-the-exemption-of-individuals-from-instapay-for-a-renewable-period-of-3-months) - so the country's flagship retail rail moved from free to priced without a published decision to price it.</details> | No change |
| Equatorial Guinea | A bank was [ordered to repay 182m CFA to 12,000 customers](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/bange_devolvera_este_2_de_abril_182_millones_a_los_funcionarios_por_recortes) after the Vice-President found an unauthorised fee scheme. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [intervention of April 2026](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/bange_devolvera_este_2_de_abril_182_millones_a_los_funcionarios_por_recortes) is the base's only recorded consumer remedy in the payment system: a bank ordered to return 182m CFA to 12,000 civil servants over fees it had no authority to charge.<br><br>It came from the Vice-President rather than a regulator or an ombudsman, and no standing complaints process is recorded. The regional layer supplies the other protection: a [deposit insurance ceiling raised in December 2025](https://www.droitmediasfinance.com/index.php/actualites/droit-bancaire/1165-cemac-i-cobac-avec-la-recente-reforme-du-fogadac-le-plafond-dindemnisation-des-deposants-passe-de-5-a-8-millions-de-fcfa).</details> | Advanced |
| Eswatini | A finance portfolio committee [tabled a report on bank transaction charges](https://times.co.sz/39920/news/cbe-called-to-probe-bank-charges/) and [recommended the central bank report a comparative benchmark](https://times.co.sz/39920/news/cbe-called-to-probe-bank-charges/) within 30 days of adoption. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The report was tabled in July 2026 ([report](https://times.co.sz/39920/news/cbe-called-to-probe-bank-charges/)) with a recommendation that the central bank produce a comparative benchmark of charges within 30 days of adoption ([benchmark](https://times.co.sz/39920/news/cbe-called-to-probe-bank-charges/)).<br><br>No adoption date, benchmark or central bank response is on record. Legislative scrutiny of transaction pricing is rare in this frame and worth recording as such; whether it produced anything is unestablished.</details> | Advanced |
| Ethiopia | The central bank moved from rule to enforcement, ordering banks and payment issuers to implement fraud-warning measures ([campaign](https://nbe.gov.et/announcements/)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The central bank launched a customer fraud awareness messaging campaign in March 2026, instructing banks, payment instrument issuers and microfinance institutions to implement fraud-warning measures under the financial consumer protection directive's own articles, in response to a rise in impersonation scams over messaging apps, text and telephone ([campaign](https://nbe.gov.et/announcements/)).<br><br>It ran its first annual financial literacy week in October 2025 under a consumer-protection theme, with outreach on mobile money use and digital risks such as fraud and data misuse ([launch](https://nbe.gov.et/nbe_news/launch-of-the-annual-financial-literacy-week-2025/)).<br><br>The operative instrument is the financial consumer protection directive issued in August 2020, which covers mobile wallets and mobile and internet banking explicitly and sets liability for unauthorised and mistaken transactions ([directive](https://justice.gov.et/wp-content/uploads/2020/08/Financial-Consumer-Protection-Directive-No.-292-2021.pdf)).<br><br>The base holds no complaint volumes, redress outcomes or fraud losses, so enforcement is visible and its effect is not.</details> | Advanced |
| Gabon | An [ordinance of 29 January 2026 fixes the rules for digital payments, and its ratification bill was heard on 22 May](https://www.focusgroupemedia.com/gabon-modernisation-de-letat-le-cadre-juridique-des-paiements-digitaux-en-examen-a-lassemblee-nationale/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The digital economy minister was heard by the National Assembly's finance committee on 22 May 2026 on the bill ratifying ordonnance 0002/PR/2026 of 29 January 2026, which fixes the rules applicable to digital payments across Gabon; the government characterises it as filling a legal vacuum with a protective framework governing online transactions and payments, aligned to the CEMAC payment services regulation of 21 December 2018](https://www.focusgroupemedia.com/gabon-modernisation-de-letat-le-cadre-juridique-des-paiements-digitaux-en-examen-a-lassemblee-nationale/).<br><br>The text of the ordinance itself was not reachable, so its thresholds, obligations and redress route cannot be read off the instrument.</details> | Advanced |
| Gambia | [Draft complaints and consumer protection regulations](https://pura.gm/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Complaints-Investigations-and-Consumer-Protection-Regulations-2026.pdf) were opened for comment in April 2026 and are not in force. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [draft regulations](https://pura.gm/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Complaints-Investigations-and-Consumer-Protection-Regulations-2026.pdf) were open for comment from 1 to 10 April 2026 and have not been made. Until they are, redress runs through the [toll-free 148 line and its escalation flow](https://pura.gm/consumer-affairs/information-education/), which is sectoral rather than a consolidated national channel.<br><br>On the payments side the monetary policy committee [ordered complaint tracking for fintechs](https://www.cbg.gm/downloads-file/c76e08c3-12ec-11f1-8725-02e599c15748) in December 2025, which is an instruction to the supervised rather than a right for the customer.</details> | Advanced |
| Ghana | A [register of licensed digital credit providers](https://www.bog.gov.gh/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Notice-No.25-BOG-SEC-GOV-2026-Notice-PUBLICATION-OF-UNLICENSED-DIGITAL-CREDIT-SERVICE-PROVIDERS-ON-THE-BANKS-WEBSITE-SOCIAL-MEDIA-PLATFORMS-AND-OTHER-COMM-CHANNELS-1.pdf) is still unpublished, a twenty-name blacklist standing in its place. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A borrower can check twenty names and cannot check whether the twenty-first is licensed; publication was undertaken in July 2026 in due course, with no date given ([blacklist](https://www.bog.gov.gh/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Notice-No.25-BOG-SEC-GOV-2026-Notice-PUBLICATION-OF-UNLICENSED-DIGITAL-CREDIT-SERVICE-PROVIDERS-ON-THE-BANKS-WEBSITE-SOCIAL-MEDIA-PLATFORMS-AND-OTHER-COMM-CHANNELS-1.pdf), [notice](https://www.bog.gov.gh/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/Notice-No.22-BOG-SEC-GOV-2026-UNLICENSED-DIGITAL-CREDIT-SERVICES-PROVIDERS-1.pdf)).<br><br>A centralised real-time layer able to block suspicious transfers as they move between networks is described as under discussion with nothing built and no operator, funder or timetable named ([fraud layer](https://www.graphic.com.gh/news/general-news/ghana-news-mtn-partners-stakeholders-to-tackle-cross-network-momo-fraud.html), [silos](https://www.myjoyonline.com/payment-service-providers-operating-in-silos-are-weakening-ghanas-fight-against-fraud-ebenezer-boffour/)).<br><br>Expansion of the financial industry security operations centre to all financial institutions including fintechs and microfinance was announced in March 2026, and four months later a digital-economy forum still listed complete onboarding as a recommendation ([operations centre](https://drive.google.com/file/d/1Lp9IUxpd5YPh2tUyN3JefO1kvFvlREzl/preview)).</details> | Mixed, a fraud layer and an operations centre are proposed while the register a borrower would need is still unpublished |
| Guinea | A consumer union [published twelve conditions](https://focusguinee.info/2026/07/26/laguiduten-salue-le-lancement-de-nimbapay-la-plateforme-nationale-de-paiement-instantane/) on the new instant payment system the day after launch, and they are unanswered. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Neither the regulator nor the operator has responded on the record ([conditions](https://focusguinee.info/2026/07/26/laguiduten-salue-le-lancement-de-nimbapay-la-plateforme-nationale-de-paiement-instantane/)).<br><br>No complaints procedure, redress mechanism, tariff transparency rule or dispute statistic exists for digital payments anywhere in the base, so an unanswered civil-society list is the whole of the consumer-protection record.</details> | Advanced |
| Guinea-Bissau | No payment consumer protection instrument appears in the base for Guinea-Bissau. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Nothing in the base records a complaints route, a redress rule or a disclosure requirement for Guinean payment users.<br><br>Authorisation is the only protection documented, and it is the union's: [six banks, one payment institution and one electronic money issuer on the register](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/inline-files/4%20-%20Liste%20Guin%C3%A9e-Bissau_2026.pdf) as at June 2026.</details> | No change |
| Kenya | [Phone-number masking](https://techcabal.com/2026/03/20/m-pesa-to-stop-sharing-full-phone-numbers-with-merchants-banks-by-end-of-2026/) went live on person-to-person transfers in March 2026, with extension to merchant and bank transfers targeted for end-2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Masking had previously been limited to the small-trader product ([masking](https://techcabal.com/2026/03/20/m-pesa-to-stop-sharing-full-phone-numbers-with-merchants-banks-by-end-of-2026/)).<br><br>It is a design change made by the operator rather than a regulatory requirement, and it addresses the same exposure — a phone number functioning as an identifier — that the High Court has since recognised in law.</details> | Advanced |
| Lesotho | [Three implementing regulations under the consumer protection Act reached Senate adoption in May 2026](https://senate.parliament.ls/2026/05/07/revised-order-paper-for-7th-may-2026/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2022-06-03 — [the Financial Consumer Protection Act establishes the regime and the central bank’s powers under it](https://centralbank.org.ls/wp-content/uploads/Financial-Consumer-Protection-Act-2022.pdf).<br><br>2026-05-07 — [three implementing regulations reached Senate adoption](https://senate.parliament.ls/2026/05/07/revised-order-paper-for-7th-may-2026/), four years after the Act.<br><br>2026-05-05 — [the Payment System Bill would license fintech providers under one law](https://www.lena.gov.ls/finance-minister-presents-payment-system-bill/).<br><br>No complaints figure, redress statistic or dispute-resolution route for payment users is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Liberia | A [central-bank-approved fee schedule](https://www.liberianobserver.com/business/africa-s-digital-payment-revolution-why-liberia-must-move-faster-on-inclusive-instant-payment-systems/article_78def9c4-1c3a-4997-80e5-76002c64d288.html) caps interoperable charges at 1.0 per cent of value to US$2,000 and a flat US$25.00 above it. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The service is available 24 hours a day ([schedule](https://www.liberianobserver.com/business/africa-s-digital-payment-revolution-why-liberia-must-move-faster-on-inclusive-instant-payment-systems/article_78def9c4-1c3a-4997-80e5-76002c64d288.html)).<br><br>The flat cap is regressive above about US$2,500 ([schedule](https://www.liberianobserver.com/business/africa-s-digital-payment-revolution-why-liberia-must-move-faster-on-inclusive-instant-payment-systems/article_78def9c4-1c3a-4997-80e5-76002c64d288.html)), where it falls below 1.0 per cent of value and keeps falling, so the schedule protects small payments and cheapens large ones.</details> | Advanced |
| Libya | Card-payment surcharges were prohibited and made enforceable by a [Municipal Guard notice](https://libyaherald.com/2026/08/municipal-guard-warns-against-imposing-surcharge-for-debit-card-payments/) in August 2026, after spreads of up to 20 per cent. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Municipal Guard issued an enforcement notice on 2 August 2026 against merchants adding a surcharge to card payments, with spreads of up to 20 per cent reported before it ([notice](https://libyaherald.com/2026/08/municipal-guard-warns-against-imposing-surcharge-for-debit-card-payments/)).<br><br>No penalty schedule, complaint route or enforcement count has been published, and the base holds no other consumer-protection measure covering electronic payment.</details> | Advanced |
| Madagascar | The central bank [opened audits of electronic money institutions](https://2424.mg/mobile-money-les-autorites-annoncent-des-audits-du-systeme-des-etablissements-de-monnaie-electronique/) in June 2026 after user complaints, and a reimbursement route followed in August. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The banking law of September 2020 establishes the supervisor's licensing, prudential and disciplinary powers over banks and electronic money providers ([law](https://www.banky-foibe.mg/admin/wp-content/uploads/2021/10/1-1-Loi-n%C2%B0-2020-011.pdf)).<br><br>The central bank governor told the National Assembly in June 2026 that audits of electronic money institutions were under way, covering technical compliance, platform security and whether issued electronic money is fully backed, and recalled the institutions' 24-hour complaint-handling obligation ([audits](https://2424.mg/mobile-money-les-autorites-annoncent-des-audits-du-systeme-des-etablissements-de-monnaie-electronique/)).<br><br>Customers who paid the same electricity bill twice by mobile money were given a reimbursement route in August 2026, with operators transmitting payment histories to the utility ([reimbursement](https://newsmada.com/2026/08/26/payer-deux-fois-la-meme-facture-les-clients-de-la-jirama-peuvent-demander-un-remboursement/)). No audit finding or sanction has been published.</details> | Advanced |
| Malawi | The regulator [found both operators in breach of the tariff-notice requirement](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/17/macra-orders-airtel-malawi-and-tnm-to-compensate-customers-over-tariff-notice-breach/) and ordered customer compensation, while a [mobile-money interoperability order](https://malawi24.com/2025/07/16/cftc-orders-tnm-mpamba-to-open-e-ticketing-platform-to-other-payment-providers/) expired unreported. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Both mobile operators were found in breach in respect of a notice window in mid-2026 and agreed to credit affected customers with bundles equal to the price difference by 31 July with evidence of compliance, alongside strengthened internal procedures; the approved price increase itself stands, so what was enforced is notice and not price ([order](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/17/macra-orders-airtel-malawi-and-tnm-to-compensate-customers-over-tariff-notice-breach/), [compliance](https://itweb.africa/article/malawi-enforces-telecom-tariff-compliance/KBpdg7pmGgaMLEew), [refund](https://www.connectingafrica.com/regulation/airtel-tnm-ordered-to-refund-malawi-customers)). The base holds no confirmation that compensation was completed.<br><br>A competition order of July 2025 required a mobile-money operator to open its exclusive football ticketing platform to other providers within a year, with concurrent physical ticketing for six months and progress reports every ninety days; the compliance year expired around July 2026 and nothing held records opening, a progress report or an enforcement step ([interoperability](https://malawi24.com/2025/07/16/cftc-orders-tnm-mpamba-to-open-e-ticketing-platform-to-other-payment-providers/)).<br><br>The communications regulator issued a public alert in September 2026 naming [fake social cash transfer messages, failed-payment pretexts redirecting money to a second number, spoofed transaction alerts, fake prizes and messaging-account takeovers, and stating that bodies running social cash transfer programmes do not use ordinary messaging to request money from beneficiaries](https://malawi24.com/2026/09/04/macra-sounds-alarm-over-rising-digital-scams/). It carries no incident count, loss figure or enforcement action.</details> | Mixed, a tariff-notice breach enforced with customer compensation while an interoperability order's compliance year passed with nothing on file |
| Mauritius | The [2018 payment systems Act](https://lawsofmauritius.govmu.org/portal/viewlegislationdocument/web/?docnumber=&doctitle=TmF0aW9uYWwgUGF5bWVudCBTeXN0ZW1zIEFjdCAyMDE4&doctype=act) is the statutory base, and [stablecoin guidance](https://www.fscmauritius.org/media/dyedhu0b/fsc-guidance-notes-on-stablecoins-and-annexure.pdf) was issued in August 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act is at its seventh consolidated version and is the statute under which payment providers are licensed and supervised ([Act](https://lawsofmauritius.govmu.org/portal/viewlegislationdocument/web/?docnumber=&doctitle=TmF0aW9uYWwgUGF5bWVudCBTeXN0ZW1zIEFjdCAyMDE4&doctype=act)).<br><br>The financial services regulator issued guidance notes on stablecoins with an annexure in August 2026, as part of its fintech series ([guidance](https://www.fscmauritius.org/media/dyedhu0b/fsc-guidance-notes-on-stablecoins-and-annexure.pdf)).<br><br>Neither instrument is a consumer redress mechanism. No complaint volume, resolution rate or compensation arrangement for payment users is held anywhere in the base.</details> | Advanced |
| Morocco | The card [interchange cap was cut to 0.50 per cent](https://conseil-concurrence.ma/communique-conjoint-du-conseil-de-la-concurrence-et-de-bank-al-maghrib/) from 1 October 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Protection here is competition remedy rather than complaint procedure. The competition authority [extended the card processor's acquiring divestiture to January and April 2026](https://conseil-concurrence.ma/communique-du-conseil-de-la-concurrence-15/), and the central bank and the authority together [cut the interchange cap to 0.50 per cent with effect from 1 October 2026](https://conseil-concurrence.ma/communique-conjoint-du-conseil-de-la-concurrence-et-de-bank-al-maghrib/).<br><br>Both act on merchant cost rather than on cardholder redress, and no payment complaints route appears in the base.</details> | Advanced |
| Mozambique | [Law 15/2026 repeals the 2008 payments law and strengthens the central bank’s licensing and supervisory powers over payment operators and fintechs](https://www.bancomoc.mz/en/media/highlights/law-no-15-2026-of-2-july-approves-the-national-payment-system-law/), and [licensing became mandatory for digital platforms from 29 December 2025](https://intic.gov.mz/mocambique-entra-hoje-numa-nova-era-digital-com-o-arranque-do-licenciamento-obrigatorio-de-plataformas-digitais-e-servicos-electronicos/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-07-02 — [a new National Payment System Law repealed the 2008 statute and strengthened the central bank's licensing, oversight and supervisory powers over payment operators and financial technology firms](https://www.bancomoc.mz/en/media/highlights/law-no-15-2026-of-2-july-approves-the-national-payment-system-law/). It is the top-level instrument governing payment-system conduct and consumer safeguards.<br><br>2025-12-29 — [mandatory registration and licensing began for all providers of electronic services and digital platforms serving the market, digital payments expressly among the covered services](https://intic.gov.mz/mocambique-entra-hoje-numa-nova-era-digital-com-o-arranque-do-licenciamento-obrigatorio-de-plataformas-digitais-e-servicos-electronicos/), with consumer data protection and service reliability stated as objectives of the regime.<br><br>Two licensing regimes now bear on the same providers, one from the central bank and one from the ICT institute, and no statement of how they relate is published. The statute's implementing regulations are not held either, so what a consumer can actually invoke is still unsettled.</details> | Advanced, a new statute replaced the 2008 payments law and platform licensing became mandatory |
| Namibia | A [determination on fees and charges for payment services](https://www.lac.org.na/laws/2025/8816.pdf) took effect on gazetting in December 2025, against [banking fraud losses of N$73.9m in 2025](https://itweb.africa/article/namibia-partners-with-banks-to-combat-fraud/Olx4zMka1eyv56km). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The determination on standards for fees and charges for payment services was issued under section 45 of the payment system statute and took effect on publication in the gazette on 24 December 2025 ([determination](https://www.lac.org.na/laws/2025/8816.pdf)).<br><br>[Losses ran at about N$8.7m a year in 2020 on the central bank's figure and reached N$73.9m for 2025, with N$65m lost between January and October](https://itweb.africa/article/namibia-partners-with-banks-to-combat-fraud/Olx4zMka1eyv56km); no 2026 figure is held.<br><br>An inaugural fraud and cybersecurity summit was convened by the ICT ministry with the central bank, the bankers' association, the incident response team and an industry cyber council, and no second edition has been reported ([summit](https://itweb.africa/article/namibia-partners-with-banks-to-combat-fraud/Olx4zMka1eyv56km)).</details> | Mixed, a fee and charge standard was gazetted while banking fraud losses rose sharply |
| Nigeria | [Fraud losses fell 51 per cent](https://punchng.com/nigerias-digital-economy-faces-trust-test/) on a reporting rate down 34 per cent, the [digital-lending regulations](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2025/09/fccpc-issues-regulations-to-tackle-digital-lending-abuses/) are under appeal, and [about 40m airtime borrowers](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/24/mtn-sees-nigeria-rebound-as-airtime-lending-returns/) sit inside the jurisdiction dispute. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Fraud losses fell to ₦25.85bn in 2025, down 51 per cent, on 67,518 incidents against 123,918 in 2021, with fraud reporting down 34 per cent in the fourth quarter ([trust](https://punchng.com/nigerias-digital-economy-faces-trust-test/), [challenge](https://techcabal.com/2026/07/29/why-trust-has-become-nigeria-digital-payments-challenge/)).<br><br>The digital consumer-lending regulations were suspended by injunction, upheld in July 2026 and enforcement resumed, with an association's appeal before the Court of Appeal; the two regulators' positions are unreconciled, one holding airtime credit a telecommunications value-added service and the other framing the regulations as its answer to harassment and data breaches, and the communications minister has directed that the existing framework stays in place, an interim settlement by direction rather than by instrument ([regulations](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2025/09/fccpc-issues-regulations-to-tackle-digital-lending-abuses/), [court](https://techcabal.com/2026/07/21/nigerias-airtime-lending-battle-ends-with-a-split-decision/), [resumption](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/22/nigeria-strengthens-consumer-protection-as-fccpc-resumes-digital-lending-enforcement/)).<br><br>The product at the centre of it reaches about 40 million users on an industry sizing of ₦300-400bn a year, and one operator resumed at about a quarter of its first-quarter run rate at a stated cost of about ₦50bn to first-half revenue ([airtime](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/24/mtn-sees-nigeria-rebound-as-airtime-lending-returns/)). A payment security operations centre is a 2028 target with no legal basis or funding line ([centre](https://thenationonlineng.net/payment-vision-2028-is-nigeria-ready-for-digital-economy/)), and a domestic cyber-insurance market is still forming ([insurance](https://itweb.africa/article/nigerias-spark-to-ignite-local-cyber-insurance-products/rxP3jMBEwAw7A2ye)).</details> | Mixed, fraud losses halved on a falling reporting rate while a digital-lending regime went to appeal and forty million airtime borrowers sat between two regulators |
| Rwanda | A [2023 regulation](https://www.rlrc.gov.rw/index.php?eID=dumpFile&f=88224&t=f&token=634b1b49ef148710eca7e00c8f396a57261f98c2) governs payment-service users, and a [baseline survey](https://www.ktpress.rw/2025/12/new-survey-reveals-consumer-digital-fraud-risks-in-rwanda/) found 97 per cent of users had experienced at least one risk. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulation made in December 2023, repealing its 2019 predecessor, covers transparency, execution, unauthorised, defective and late transactions, refunds and liability, security and complaints ([regulation](https://www.rlrc.gov.rw/index.php?eID=dumpFile&f=88224&t=f&token=634b1b49ef148710eca7e00c8f396a57261f98c2)).<br><br>A baseline survey reported in December 2025 found 97 per cent of surveyed digital financial service users had experienced at least one risk and 84 per cent reported fraud, with participants agreeing to develop a national action plan ([survey](https://www.ktpress.rw/2025/12/new-survey-reveals-consumer-digital-fraud-risks-in-rwanda/)).<br><br>A regulation in force for two years and those figures are the two halves of this indicator, and they do not agree.</details> | Advanced |
| Sierra Leone | A [national fraud roadmap was validated](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/20/sierra-leone-validates-national-roadmap-to-tackle-mobile-and-digital-fraud/) in August 2026, and [no rule allocating the loss](https://sierraleoneconcordtimes.com/government-unveils-multi-agency-strategy-to-tackle-rising-cyber-fraud/) is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The roadmap was validated at the close of a two-day workshop convening the operator, two ministries, the regulator and the central bank on registration, verification compliance and fraud reporting ([roadmap](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/20/sierra-leone-validates-national-roadmap-to-tackle-mobile-and-digital-fraud/), [workshop](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/actualites/finance/sierra-leone-vers-une-feuille-de-route-contre-la-fraude-liee-au-mobile-money), [strategy](https://sierraleoneconcordtimes.com/government-unveils-multi-agency-strategy-to-tackle-rising-cyber-fraud/)). Its text, costing and timetable are not held, and the gap it addresses is unchanged: no liability rule, refund duty or reversal mechanism is proposed, so the consumer's position after a fraud is exactly where it was ([alert](https://sierraloaded.sl/news/salima-bah-government-on-mobile-money-fraud/), [parliament](https://www.sierraleonemonitor.com/parliament-orange-money-scams/)).<br><br>Two published analyses disagree on whether the problem is absent law or unenforced law, and the disagreement is unreconciled ([scam](https://owlpress-sl.com/dont-call-meeting-inside-whatsapp-scam-emptying-orange-money/), [enforcement](https://sierraloaded.sl/news/sylvia-blyden-kyc-mobile-money-fraud/), [allocation](https://sierraleoneconcordtimes.com/government-unveils-multi-agency-strategy-to-tackle-rising-cyber-fraud/)).<br><br>Complaint volumes are operator-reported in a ministerial statement with no series and no earlier figure ([complaints](https://sierraleoneconcordtimes.com/government-unveils-multi-agency-strategy-to-tackle-rising-cyber-fraud/)), and the operator's own scam-alert tool publishes no report volume, response time, remedy or referral route ([tool](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/18/orange-sierra-leone-launches-scam-alert-tool-to-combat-fraud/)).</details> | Mixed, a multi-agency fraud roadmap validated while no rule allocates the loss and the consumer's position after a fraud is unchanged |
| Somalia | A [telecommunications consumer protection regulation](https://nca.gov.so/resources/consultations) opened for statutory public comment in July 2026; nothing equivalent covers payments. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator's consultations register shows the draft regulation published for public comment on 8 July 2026, closing 31 August, under a statutory 45-day process ([register](https://nca.gov.so/resources/consultations)). It covers pricing and contract transparency, service quality, operator obligations and enforcement.<br><br>A stakeholder review preceded it in February 2026 ([consultation](https://nca.gov.so/nca-holds-consultation-on-telecom-consumer-protection-regulation/)).<br><br>For payments the operative protections remain the 2020 mobile money regulations' safeguarding and conduct-of-business provisions ([regulations](https://centralbank.gov.so/wp-content/uploads/2025/06/Mobile-Money-Regulation-2020_amended_2021.pdf)); the bill that would give the central bank a supervisory framework is unsigned.</details> | Advanced |
| South Africa | Enforcement rose to [140 public warnings and R2.89bn in penalties in 2025/26](https://www.fsca.co.za/_api/cr3ad_researchanddatadocses%280a08fd4a-2d8f-f111-8076-70a8a52afc83%29/cr3ad_document/$value), while the licensing regime for crypto providers [is stated not to reach decentralised finance](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/sas-crypto-rules-hit-decentralised-finance-wall/KA3Ww7dzpmnqrydZ). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-07/08 — the conduct regulator's fourth actions report [put 140 public warnings and R2.89bn of penalties on 76 persons for 2025/26, against 107 warnings and R119.8m the year before](https://www.fsca.co.za/_api/cr3ad_researchanddatadocses%280a08fd4a-2d8f-f111-8076-70a8a52afc83%29/cr3ad_document/$value). [Most warnings concerned unregistered providers promoting investment offerings on social media, and about 20% involved impersonation of licensed institutions, the regulator and its own staff among them](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/social-media-is-sas-biggest-financial-scam-vehicle/8OKdWMDXxQrMbznQ). [The penalty jump is driven by one deepfake-advertising matter of over R2bn](https://www.fsca.co.za/_api/cr3ad_researchanddatadocses%280a08fd4a-2d8f-f111-8076-70a8a52afc83%29/cr3ad_document/$value), whose reconsiderations were pending at the report date, so the total is not a run rate.<br><br>2026-08-11 — the perimeter is stated and it has a hole in it: licensing of centralised crypto-asset service providers is in force, but the regulator [says the model has no purchase on decentralised finance — protocols with no company, board or natural person to hold accountable](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/sas-crypto-rules-hit-decentralised-finance-wall/KA3Ww7dzpmnqrydZ) — and plans to attach obligations elsewhere. No instrument for that is published.<br><br>2026-04 — separately, [amendment regulations shifted the anti-spam duty in direct marketing to the consumer commission](https://www.polity.org.za/article/do-not-call-me-ill-call-you-south-africas-2026-cpa-amendment-regulations-operationalising-the-national-optout-regime-for-direct-marketing-and-shifting-daytoday-antispam-responsibility-to-the-national-consumer-commission-2026-04-20).</details> | Advanced |
| Sudan | Two central bank circulars made in 2026 — on [USSD services](https://cbos.gov.sd/ar/node/24567/) and on [anti-money-laundering controls](https://cbos.gov.sd/sites/default/files/%D9%85%D9%86%D8%B4%D9%88%D8%B1%20%D8%B1%D9%82%D9%85%288-2026%29-%20%D8%B6%D9%88%D8%A7%D8%A8%D8%B7%20%D9%85%D9%83%D8%A7%D9%81%D8%AD%D8%A9%20%D8%BA%D8%B3%D9%84%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D9%85%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%84.pdf) — set binding duties for the first time. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Circular 7/2026 of 31 March sets minimum requirements for USSD financial services: fee disclosure, no debit on failed transactions, mandatory transaction notifications, a round-the-clock complaint centre, 48-hour resolution, masking of sensitive account data, two-factor authentication for high-value transactions and encryption to TLS 1.2 and ISO/IEC 27001 ([circular](https://cbos.gov.sd/ar/node/24567/)).<br><br>Circular 8/2026 of 30 April extends monitoring, exception reporting, funding and value limits and service suspension to mobile and phone payment channels ([circular](https://cbos.gov.sd/sites/default/files/%D9%85%D9%86%D8%B4%D9%88%D8%B1%20%D8%B1%D9%82%D9%85%288-2026%29-%20%D8%B6%D9%88%D8%A7%D8%A8%D8%B7%20%D9%85%D9%83%D8%A7%D9%81%D8%AD%D8%A9%20%D8%BA%D8%B3%D9%84%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D9%85%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%84.pdf)).<br><br>Enforcement followed: a public alert against an unlicensed application in South Darfur ([alert](https://cbos.gov.sd/sites/default/files/%D8%AA%D8%B9%D9%85%D9%8A%D9%85_0.pdf)) and a licence revoked in July ([revocation](https://sudanhorizon.com/cbos-officially-revokes-al-asjad-companys-license-following-review/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Tanzania | [Per-instrument fee caps](https://www.mwananchi.co.tz/mw/habari/biashara/makato-kikwazo-kuelekea-malipo-ya-kidijitali-5542380) were set with card-scheme negotiations moving the card fee from payer to merchant, and the governor [rejected a general price cap](https://www.mwananchi.co.tz/mw/habari/biashara/makato-kikwazo-kuelekea-malipo-ya-kidijitali-5542380) saying competition will cut fees within six months. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The caps are per-instrument rather than a general tariff ([caps](https://www.mwananchi.co.tz/mw/habari/biashara/makato-kikwazo-kuelekea-malipo-ya-kidijitali-5542380), [interoperability](https://www.ippmedia.co.tz/the-guardian/news/local-news/read/interoperability-costs-cut-while-113-agencies-operate-payments-systems-2026-04-28-111407)).<br><br>The general cap was rejected on the ground that charges are already low; an analyst put the worked cost at about TZS 3,500 per TZS 100,000 paid and argued the mandate manufactures avoidance ([rejection](https://www.mwananchi.co.tz/mw/habari/biashara/makato-kikwazo-kuelekea-malipo-ya-kidijitali-5542380)).<br><br>Financial consumer protection amendment regulations moved the dormant-account period from five to twelve months, set complaint maxima of twelve hours for mobile money and fourteen days for credit, and fines of TZS 1m a day; the data-protection cross-reference is what wires the payments regime into the data regime ([regulations](https://kemiadvocates.co.tz/2025/06/27/legal-update-key-highlights-of-the-bank-of-tanzania-financial-consumer-protection-amendment-regulations-gn-no-298-of-2025-date-23-may-2025)).</details> | Mixed, per-instrument fee caps set and a general cap rejected while the regulations tighten complaint deadlines |
| Togo | A [national consumer-rights outreach tour](https://atop.tg/telecommunications-larcep-echange-avec-des-consommateurs-des-savanes-sur-leurs-droits-et-obligations/) reached its seventh edition across August 2026, covering mobile-money fraud and service satisfaction, with no attendance, complaint or outcome figure published. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The seventh edition ran 3 to 28 August 2026 through the regions, with stages covering mobile-money fraud and consumer satisfaction ([tour](https://atop.tg/telecommunications-larcep-echange-avec-des-consommateurs-des-savanes-sur-leurs-droits-et-obligations/), [fraud](https://atop.tg/bassar-telecommunications-les-consommateurs-sensibilises-sur-leurs-droits-et-obligations/), [satisfaction](https://plateaux-actu.tg/atakpame-larcep-poursuit-sa-tournee-nationale-de-sensibilisation-aupres-des-consommateurs-des-services-de-communications-electroniques/), [regions](https://atop.tg/kozah-communications-electroniques-larcep-edifie-les-consommateurs-de-kozah-assoli-et-binah-sur-leurs-droits-et-obligations/)).<br><br>No attendance figure, complaint volume or outcome measure for the tour is published. Seven editions of a regulator-run outreach programme with no published complaint series is the shape of consumer protection here.</details> | Advanced |
| Tunisia | A published analysis finds [no rule allocating the loss](https://www.tunisiaonlinenews.com/tunisia-cyber-risk-who-pays-when-electronic-payments-fail/) between bank, merchant and cardholder when an electronic payment is hijacked or blocked by a cyber incident. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The analysis checks the 2000 electronic commerce law, the 2023 cybersecurity decree-law and the central bank's 2024 circular and finds no text allocating the loss ([analysis](https://www.tunisiaonlinenews.com/tunisia-cyber-risk-who-pays-when-electronic-payments-fail/)).<br><br>It sets that against a payments market of 164.8 million transactions worth 29.5bn dinars in 2025, up 12.3 per cent, and reported cyber incidents rising from 63,000 in 2021 to more than 155,000 in 2022 ([market](https://www.tunisiaonlinenews.com/tunisia-cyber-risk-who-pays-when-electronic-payments-fail/)).<br><br>A market of that size with no loss-allocation rule is the same gap SLE records, and here it is identified by a legal analysis rather than by a fraud wave.</details> | No change |
| Uganda | A [National Financial Inclusion Strategy 2023-2028](https://ugandabankers.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/01/National-Financial-Inclusion-Strategy-2023-2028-.pdf) was published in November 2023 and nothing in the base moves it. <details><summary>Full record</summary>It was published in November 2023 ([strategy](https://ugandabankers.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/01/National-Financial-Inclusion-Strategy-2023-2028-.pdf)).<br><br>No mid-term review, target position or consumer-protection instrument under it appears anywhere in this ledger, against a payments record that includes a stablecoin corridor, a tokenised exchange, buy-now-pay-later device credit with no published default figure, and digital lending regulation that exists only as a stated intention.</details> | No change |
| Zambia | The [2026 payment Act carries a dedicated consumer-protection regime](https://zambialii.org/akn/zm/act/2026/5/eng@2026-04-08), over a [2018 directive prohibiting 26 categories of charge](https://www.boz.zm/sites/default/files/2026-03/public_notice_on_the_prohibition_charge_1.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[A central bank directive prohibits 26 categories of unwarranted charges and fees on financial services](https://www.boz.zm/sites/default/files/2026-03/public_notice_on_the_prohibition_charge_1.pdf) — the clearest standalone consumer-protection instrument for digital and card payments, and eight years old.<br><br>2026-03 — [the central bank's journal carried a notice on registration of virtual asset service providers](https://www.boz.zm/sites/default/files/2026-06/Zambanker_March_2026.pdf), moving crypto activity toward supervised status.<br><br>2026-04-08 — [the new payment system Act carries a dedicated consumer-protection regime alongside market-conduct rules for payment service providers](https://zambialii.org/akn/zm/act/2026/5/eng@2026-04-08). It is not yet commenced. No complaint volume, redress statistic or enforcement action under the 2018 directive is held, so what protection currently delivers cannot be read.</details> | Advanced |
| Zimbabwe | [Bank charge caps](https://www.newsday.co.zw/local-news/article/200052094/rbz-cuts-limits-bank-charges-expands-mobile-and-zipit-limits) were directed for compliance by March 2026, with a named analyst recording the governor conceding limited enforcement capacity. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The caps were set in the February 2026 monetary policy statement ([caps](https://www.newsday.co.zw/local-news/article/200052094/rbz-cuts-limits-bank-charges-expands-mobile-and-zipit-limits), [consumer rights](https://zimbabwenow.co.zw/articles/12707/electronic-transactions-a-consumer-right-consumer-protection-commission), [legal tender](https://www.newsday.co.zw/opinion-analysis/article/200055031/zig-is-legal-tender-so-why-cant-you-use-it-online)).<br><br>Online payment gateways are configured to settle in United States dollars first, so local currency is not accepted on them; that is named-analyst opinion, attributed ([gateways](https://www.newsday.co.zw/opinion-analysis/article/200055031/zig-is-legal-tender-so-why-cant-you-use-it-online)).<br><br>A price cap whose own setter concedes limited enforcement capacity, and a legal tender that online gateways will not take, are the two consumer findings this ledger supports.</details> | Advanced |
